<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01T3htRNtCa6UUWrBKKfjFtb`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models report high Collision Avoidance Rates (CAR) on safety benchmarks, yet we show these metrics are contaminated by action collapse.
3	On SafeLIBERO, an SDF-augmented Pi0 achieves 91.5\% CAR while 99.6\% of its safe episodes exhibit end-effector displacement below 0.1\,mm---the robot is frozen, not safe.
4	We present displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes sub-threshold episodes, as a corrective metric; the bimodal displacement distribution makes it robust across a $[2, 200]$\,mm threshold range.
5	Our analysis further reveals that end-effector-only Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision is neither necessary nor structurally sufficient for collision avoidance.
6	It is counterproductive at inference time: three ground-truth oracle variants all reduce dfCAR below unrefined baselines, as SDF gradients repelling the end-effector drive arm links into obstacles.
7	Its contribution during training is dose-dependent: LoRA-only fine-tuning raises dfCAR from 26.8\% to 54.83\%$\,\pm\,$2.25, matching SDF-augmented training at $\lambda{=}0.005$ (54.5\%$\,\pm\,$2.29) and $\lambda{=}0.01$ (56.33\%$\,\pm\,$1.61); at $\lambda{=}0.1$, two of three seeds show a marginal signal (58.25\%$\,\pm\,$0.35, $p{\approx}0.039$) whose per-task avoidance pattern still mirrors the demonstration distribution, requiring further investigation.
8	Per-task avoidance patterns remain identical across all conditions, indicating that collision avoidance arises from collision-free demonstration data rather than EE-only geometric supervision.
9	Displacement filtering is necessary for meaningful VLA safety evaluation; EE-only geometric supervision can be neither credited with nor expected to deliver collision avoidance.
10	\end{abstract}
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01TEPJHiBXjv1aTGQgBtKrg7`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}
2	
3	Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models must avoid collisions to operate safely in physical environments~\cite{li2026vlasafety}. The SafeLIBERO benchmark~\cite{hu2025vlsa} provides the first systematic protocol for measuring collision avoidance in VLA manipulation, and recent methods, including SDF-augmented training~\cite{zhang2025safevla}, constraint-based planning~\cite{yang2025cape}, and collision-free demonstrations~\cite{cofreeevla2026}, report high Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). Our SDF-augmented Pi0~\cite{black2024pi0} reaches 91.5\% CAR across 800 episodes. The problem appears close to solved.
4	
5	\begin{figure}[t]
6	\centering
7	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{fig1_teaser}
8	\caption{The fake safety problem in VLA collision avoidance evaluation. Standard CAR classifies a frozen robot as safe because it produces zero collisions (left). dfCAR filters out action-collapsed episodes where displacement falls below $\tau_d$, revealing that Pi0+A1's 91.5\% safety score drops to ${\approx}$\,0\% (center). Pi0.5 exhibits genuine avoidance with active motion above 288\,mm in all safe episodes, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (right).}
9	\label{fig:teaser}
10	\end{figure}
11	
12	It is not (Figure~\ref{fig:teaser}). Of the 732 collision-free episodes, 99.6\% exhibit displacement below 0.1\,mm; TSR is 0\% across all 800 episodes. The robot is frozen, not safe. The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \emph{fake safety}: \emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.
13	
14	We present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.
15	
16	\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\% to ${\approx}$\,0\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\,=\,74.7\%, CAR\,=\,26.8\%, with every safe episode above 288\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\ref{sec:fake_safety}).
17	
18	\textbf{EE-only SDF refinement is structurally counterproductive.} Three ground-truth oracle variants all achieve \emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines (73.1--76.9\% vs.\ 82--83\%). The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\ref{sec:ee_only}).
19	
20	\textbf{Collision-free demonstrations are sufficient for collision avoidance.} LoRA fine-tuning on Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\sim}$28 points (26.8\% $\to$ 54.83\%) with zero frozen episodes; adding SDF supervision at low loss weights ($\lambda \in \{0.005, 0.01\}$) produces no measurable benefit and at high $\lambda = 0.1$ yields only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($p \approx 0.039$, $n = 2$); the per-task avoidance pattern is identical with and without geometric supervision, indicating that the collision-free demonstration distribution, not EE-only SDF supervision, accounts for all observed avoidance learning on this benchmark (Section~\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).
21	
22	Our contributions are:
23	\begin{itemize}
24	    \item \textbf{dfCAR metric:} displacement-filtered CAR that separates genuine avoidance from action collapse, robust across $\tau_d \in [2, 200]$\,mm.
25	    \item \textbf{Fake safety diagnosis:} 100+ experiments across Pi0/Pi0.5 showing $>$99\% of Pi0 safe episodes are action-collapse artifacts.
26	    \item \textbf{EE-only SDF is counterproductive:} ground-truth oracle refinement \emph{reduces} CAR below unrefined baselines by redistributing collisions to unmonitored arm links.
27	    \item \textbf{Demonstration sufficiency:} on SafeLIBERO, collision-free demonstrations account for all measured CAR gains (${\sim}$28\,pp); EE-only SDF at low $\lambda$ ($\leq 0.01$) adds nothing, and at high $\lambda = 0.1$ shows only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($n = 2$) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.
28	\end{itemize}
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01UuzK6uGRWS3L5ginBEhq97`</summary>

```text
1	\section{The Fake Safety Problem}\label{sec:fake_safety}
2	
3	Standard CAR assumes that collision-free episodes reflect deliberate avoidance. This assumption is systematically violated: in every Pi0 configuration tested, over 99\% of collision-free episodes are action-collapse artifacts (Table~\ref{tab:fake_safety}).\footnote{Pi0-family values report obstacle displacement from the original protocol; Pi0.5 reports EE displacement from the rotation-corrected protocol (\S\ref{sec:dfcar}). Sub-millimeter displacement indicates action collapse in both cases.} Applying dfCAR collapses all three to ${\approx}$\,0\%. Pi0+A1 achieves the \emph{highest} raw CAR (91.5\%) because more complete action collapse (from a parameter-name mismatch preventing LoRA loading; Table~\ref{tab:configurations}) yields higher fake safety. Pi0.5 validates the metric from the opposite direction: every safe episode exceeds 200\,mm displacement, yielding dfCAR equal to raw CAR (26.8\%\,$\pm$\,1.6). The displacement distribution is sharply bimodal (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_displacement}), with a five-order-of-magnitude gap that makes $\tau_d$ robust.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\caption{Fake safety decomposition on SafeLIBERO. dfCAR excludes episodes with displacement $< \tau_d = 0.1$\,mm.}
8	\label{tab:fake_safety}
9	\vspace{2pt}
10	\small
11	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
12	\toprule
13	Method & Ep. & CAR (\%) & TSR (\%) & Fake Safe (\%) & dfCAR (\%) \\
14	\midrule
15	Pi0 Full-FT  & 200 & 83.0 & 0.5 & 99.4 & $\approx$\,0 \\
16	Pi0 LoRA     & 200 & 82.0 & 0.0 & ${\sim}$99 & $\approx$\,0 \\
17	Pi0+A1 (SDF) & 800 & 91.5 & 0.0 & 99.6 & $\approx$\,0$^*$ \\
18	\midrule
19	Pi0.5 (3-seed)  & 600 & 26.8\,$\pm$\,1.6 & 74.7\,$\pm$\,1.5 & 0.0 & 26.8 \\
20	\bottomrule
21	\end{tabular}
22	\vspace{1pt}
23	{\footnotesize $^*$\,3/732 safe episodes above threshold; denominator too small for reliable rate.}
24	\end{table}
25	
26	Figure~\ref{fig:tsr_car_scatter} reveals spatial structure in the TSR-CAR plane.\label{sec:anticorrelation} Pi0-family models cluster in the upper-left (high CAR, near-zero TSR), Pi0.5 in the lower-right (high TSR, low CAR). The upper-right quadrant (high TSR \emph{and} CAR) represents genuinely safe behavior; the upper-left is accessible through action collapse alone. Under dfCAR, Pi0 configurations drop to the origin, revealing that the apparent safety-capability tradeoff was an artifact of action-collapse contamination.
27	
28	\begin{figure}[t]
29	\centering
30	\includegraphics[width=0.85\linewidth]{fig_tsr_car_scatter.pdf}
31	\caption{TSR vs.\ CAR for all configurations. Pi0-family models cluster in the upper-left (high CAR from inaction), Pi0.5 in the lower-right (high TSR with collision exposure), fine-tuned Pi0.5 in the center. The upper-right quadrant (high TSR and CAR) represents genuinely safe behavior. Error bars: $\pm$1 std across seeds.}
32	\label{fig:tsr_car_scatter}
33	\end{figure}

1	\section{End-Effector-Only SDF Cannot Ensure Full-Body Safety}\label{sec:ee_only}
2	
3	Could a perfect SDF signal enable effective EE-only refinement? We replace the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry (\S\ref{sec:oracle_setup}). Table~\ref{tab:oracle} and Figure~\ref{fig:oracle_bar} show that all three oracle variants achieve \emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines: the best oracle (76.9\%) falls 5.1 points below LoRA (82.0\%). Refinement with perfect distance information produces more collisions, not fewer.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\caption{Oracle SDF refinement with ground-truth distance fields. All oracle variants achieve lower CAR than unrefined baselines. Per-task breakdown in Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}.}
8	\label{tab:oracle}
9	\vspace{2pt}
10	\small
11	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
12	\toprule
13	Method & CAR (\%) & Fake Safe (\%) & Refines/ep \\
14	\midrule
15	LoRA baseline (no refine) & 82.0 & 99.4 & -- \\
16	Full-FT baseline (no refine) & 83.0 & -- & -- \\
17	\midrule
18	Oracle-adaptive (GT, dyn.\ $\alpha$) & 75.0 & 98.3 & 14.2 \\
19	Oracle-fixed (GT, $\alpha{=}0.005$) & 73.1 & 98.3 & 14.8 \\
20	Oracle-feature (GT, feat.\ space) & 76.9 & 96.7 & 241.7 \\
21	\midrule
22	A3 learned SDF & 77.1 & -- & 0.0 \\
23	\bottomrule
24	\end{tabular}
25	\end{table}
26	
27	\begin{figure}[t]
28	\centering
29	\includegraphics[width=0.85\linewidth]{fig_oracle_bar.pdf}
30	\caption{CAR for oracle SDF refinement versus unrefined baselines. All GT oracle variants underperform baselines despite perfect distance information.}
31	\label{fig:oracle_bar}
32	\end{figure}
33	
34	Per-task analysis reveals the mechanism (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}). On the one task where the EE is the primary collision body, oracle refinement helps (+9 points). On the remaining three, the SDF gradient pushes the EE away from obstacles but rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes, trading one collision for another. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps per episode). EE-only refinement is structurally counterproductive: joint-space coupling between EE displacement and arm-link rotation redistributes collisions to unmonitored links regardless of signal quality.
```

```text
1	\section{Avoidance Source Diagnosis}\label{sec:avoidance_source}
2	
3	Fine-tuning on collision-free demonstrations produces genuine collision avoidance: LoRA-adapted Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\sim}$28 percentage points with zero frozen episodes. Does SDF geometric supervision contribute additional avoidance beyond what demonstrations provide? We compare LoRA-only, LoRA+SDF ($\lambda = 0.005$), and LoRA+SDF ($\lambda = 0.01$) on Pi0.5, all evaluated at the 30K-step checkpoint.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\caption{Avoidance source attribution on SafeLIBERO. SDF at $\lambda \leq 0.01$ adds no measurable CAR beyond LoRA-only ($p > 0.1$); $\lambda = 0.1$ shows a marginal signal ($z = 1.76$, $p \approx 0.039$, $n{=}2$) that requires additional seeds. All fine-tuned models exhibit 0\% frozen rate and displacement $>$\,0.37\,m.}
8	\label{tab:avoidance_source}
9	\vspace{2pt}
10	\small
11	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
12	\toprule
13	Method & Seeds & TSR (\%) & CAR (\%) & EE Disp (m) \\
14	\midrule
15	Pi0.5 baseline & 3 & 74.7\,$\pm$\,1.5 & 26.8\,$\pm$\,1.6 & 0.367 \\
16	\midrule
17	LoRA-only (no SDF) & 3 & 49.7\,$\pm$\,3.2 & 54.83\,$\pm$\,2.25 & 0.391 \\
18	LoRA+SDF $\lambda{=}0.005$ & 3 & 54.0\,$\pm$\,3.0 & 54.5\,$\pm$\,2.29 & 0.387 \\
19	LoRA+SDF $\lambda{=}0.01$ & 3 & 53.8\,$\pm$\,1.5 & 56.33\,$\pm$\,1.61 & 0.389 \\
20	LoRA+SDF $\lambda{=}0.1$\textsuperscript{$\dagger$} & 2 & 48.0\,$\pm$\,1.4 & 58.25\,$\pm$\,0.35 & 0.394 \\
21	\bottomrule
22	\end{tabular}
23	\vspace{1pt}
24	{\footnotesize \textsuperscript{$\dagger$}30K checkpoint, two of three seeds; third seed in progress.}
25	\end{table}
26	
27	Table~\ref{tab:avoidance_source} shows that collision-free demonstrations alone raise CAR by ${\sim}$28 percentage points (26.8\% $\to$ 54.83\%) with zero frozen episodes and displacement above 0.37\,m, confirming genuine avoidance. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit: LoRA-only CAR (54.83\%) is within 0.25\,pp of $\lambda = 0.005$ and statistically indistinguishable from $\lambda = 0.01$ (56.33\%, $p > 0.1$; Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_null}). A Phase~2 ablation ($\lambda = 0$ after step 5K) yields CAR\,=\,57.5\%\footnote{Single seed (s2); computational constraints precluded additional seeds for this ablation.}, confirming that SDF gradients produce no lasting behavioral effect. The per-task CAR ordering is preserved across all conditions (chocolate\_pudding highest, bbq\_sauce lowest) and invariant to $\lambda$ across a 20$\times$ range, indicating that avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.
28	
29	\paragraph{CAR--TSR trade-off.}\label{sec:tsr_collapse}
30	Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\sim}$21--25\,pp (74.7\% $\to$ 49.7--54.0\%), reflecting distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\,0.37\,m displacement and 0\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}), confirming that among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.
31	
32	\paragraph{Dose-response at higher loss weights.}\label{sec:lambda_high}
33	Increasing $\lambda$ by 20$\times$ to 0.1 yields CAR\,=\,58.25\%\,$\pm$\,0.35 at the 30K checkpoint (two of three seeds), above the null distribution (mean 54.67\%, $\sigma = 2.04$, $z = 1.76$, $p \approx 0.039$). This departure is suggestive but inconclusive at $n = 2$. A stepwise ablation across six checkpoints (16K--28K) shows no sustained trend (mean 55.3\%, within 0.3$\sigma$ of the null; Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_stepwise}), and per-task CAR ordering at 30K remains identical to all other conditions (Figure~\ref{fig:lambda_car}). The response is thus dose-dependent: SDF is fully redundant at $\lambda \leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$), with a marginal signal at $\lambda = 0.1$ whose modest effect size (${\sim}$3.6\,pp) and limited sample preclude strong conclusions. Even at elevated $\lambda$, avoidance patterns remain driven by demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.
34	
35	\begin{figure}[t]
36	\centering
37	\includegraphics[width=0.85\linewidth]{fig5_lambda_curve}
38	\caption{Effect of SDF loss weight $\lambda$ on CAR and TSR. $\lambda = 0$: LoRA-only. Low $\lambda$ ($\leq 0.01$) remains within the null band; $\lambda = 0.1$ (58.25\%, 2 seeds) shows a marginal departure ($p \approx 0.039$) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.}
39	\label{fig:lambda_car}
40	\end{figure}
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Across 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes on two VLA model families, three findings converge: current VLA safety evaluation is structurally unreliable. Action collapse renders reported CAR illusory (\S\ref{sec:dfcar}); EE-only oracle refinement is counterproductive (\S\ref{sec:ee_only}); and collision-free demonstrations, not geometric supervision, account for all measured avoidance gains (\S\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).
4	
5	\paragraph{Why EE-only SDF fails.}
6	Two independent causes explain the null result. \textit{(i)~Demonstration data subsumes the SDF signal.} Collision-free demonstrations implicitly encode spatial avoidance; the ${\sim}$28\,pp CAR gain arises entirely from this distributional shift, with per-task ordering invariant across all SDF conditions (\S\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). At low $\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\,pp below unrefined baselines (\S\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. These levels are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.
7	
8	\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}
9	dfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\sim}$658K trainable), confirming it is a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot.
10	
11	\paragraph{Limitations.}
12	Our evaluation covers one benchmark suite (SafeLIBERO, four tasks) and two model families (Pi0, Pi0.5). The tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulator and known obstacles may not generalize to mobile platforms and cluttered scenes. Oracle experiments used a largely frozen model (Pi0+A1, TSR${\approx}$0\%); testing on an actively moving policy would better isolate the structural limitation. The $\lambda = 0.1$ condition has $n = 2$ seeds; the marginal departure ($p \approx 0.039$) is inconclusive. Full-body SDF remains untested. Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\sim}$21--25\,pp, yet SSR/TSR increases from 0.33 to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}), ruling out reduced exposure as the sole explanation.
13	
14	\paragraph{Future work.}
15	Three directions follow: (1) full-body SDF supervision along the kinematic chain; (2) cross-architecture evaluation on OpenVLA, RT-2, and other VLA families; (3) planning-layer safety constraints in joint space rather than EE space.
16	
17	\paragraph{Broader impact.}
18	Contaminated CAR scores allow models with no genuine avoidance to pass safety benchmarks. dfCAR exposes this gap with minimal changes to existing protocols.
19	
```

```text
1	\section{Experimental Setup and Metrics}\label{sec:method}
2	
3	\subsection{Benchmark and Models}\label{sec:benchmark}
4	
5	We evaluate on SafeLIBERO~\cite{hu2025vlsa}, the \textit{object\_pudding} suite (Level~I): four tabletop pick-and-place tasks with collision hazards, using per-timestep collision detection. Two VLA families are tested: \textbf{Pi0} (${\sim}$2.5B)~\cite{black2024pi0} with flow matching~\cite{lipman2023flow}, and \textbf{Pi0.5} with broader pre-training. Table~\ref{tab:configurations} lists all configurations. Each uses 50 episodes per task (200 total); Pi0.5 multi-seed runs use 3 seeds (600 episodes). We report Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR), the fraction of collision-free episodes, and Task Success Rate (TSR), the fraction achieving task completion~\cite{hu2025vlsa,zhang2025safevla}.
6	
7	\begin{table}[t]
8	\centering
9	\caption{Training configurations. Trainable parameters exclude the frozen backbone.}
10	\label{tab:configurations}
11	\vspace{2pt}
12	\small
13	\begin{tabular}{llr}
14	\toprule
15	Configuration & Description & Trainable Params \\
16	\midrule
17	Pi0 Full-FT & Full fine-tune, flow-matching loss & ${\sim}$2.5B \\
18	Pi0 LoRA & LoRA adapters only & ${\sim}$33.5M \\
19	Pi0+A1 (SDF aux)\textsuperscript{$\dagger$} & LoRA + SDF decoder + EE extractor & ${\sim}$658K \\
20	Pi0+A3 (learned SDF) & LoRA + SDF decoder w/ gradient refinement & ${\sim}$34.2M \\
21	Pi0.5 (pre-trained) & No fine-tuning & 0 \\
22	Pi0.5 LoRA & LoRA fine-tune on Pi0.5 & ${\sim}$33.5M \\
23	Pi0.5+A1 (SDF aux) & LoRA + SDF aux on Pi0.5, $\lambda \in \{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\}$ & ${\sim}$34.2M \\
24	\bottomrule
25	\end{tabular}
26	\vspace{2pt}
27	\raggedright\footnotesize\textsuperscript{$\dagger$}LoRA weights not loaded due to parameter-name mismatch; only auxiliary heads trained.
28	\end{table}
29	
30	\subsection{Displacement-Filtered CAR}\label{sec:dfcar}
31	
32	Standard CAR conflates genuine avoidance with action collapse. We define an episode as \emph{action-collapsed} when $d_{\max}(e) < \tau_d$ ($\tau_d = 0.1$\,mm) and exclude such episodes:
33	\begin{equation}\label{eq:car_f}
34	\text{dfCAR} = \frac{|\{e : \text{safe}(e) \;\land\; d_{\max}(e) \geq \tau_d\}|}{|\{e : d_{\max}(e) \geq \tau_d\}|}
35	\end{equation}
36	The displacement distribution is sharply bimodal, with collapsed episodes below 1\,$\mu$m and active episodes above 200\,mm; any $\tau_d \in [2, 200]$\,mm yields identical results (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_threshold}).\footnote{See Section~\ref{sec:fake_safety} for details on the displacement measurement protocol.}
37	
38	\subsection{SDF Auxiliary Training and Oracle Refinement}\label{sec:sdf_aux}
39	
40	We augment LoRA-adapted~\cite{hu2022lora} VLAs with an SDF decoder and an EE position extractor (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_training}, Table~\ref{tab:hyperparams}). The total loss is:
41	\begin{equation}\label{eq:total_loss}
42	\mathcal{L}_\text{total} = \mathcal{L}_\text{action} + \lambda \left( \mathcal{L}_\text{SDF} + \mathcal{L}_\text{EE} \right)
43	\end{equation}
44	where $\lambda \in \{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\}$. In Pi0+A1, a parameter-name mismatch prevented LoRA loading, causing action collapse (TSR\,=\,0\%; Table~\ref{tab:configurations}). To test the upper bound of EE-only refinement (Section~\ref{sec:ee_only}), we also replace the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry in three oracle variants (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_oracle}).\label{sec:oracle_setup}
45

```text
1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related}
2	
3	\paragraph{Vision-Language-Action Models.}
4	VLA models combine pretrained vision-language backbones with action prediction for generalist manipulation~\cite{brohan2023rt2,black2024pi0,chi2023diffusionpolicy,kim2024openvla}. Recent work enhances spatial reasoning through auxiliary 3D supervision~\cite{li2025spatialforcing,li2025qdepth,depthvla2025}, but safety considerations remain largely absent: most VLAs train on collision-free demonstrations and evaluate task success alone, with no mechanism to distinguish genuine avoidance from incidental collision-free trajectories.
5	
6	\paragraph{Safety in Robot Learning.}
7	Safety-aware approaches span training-time constrained RL~\cite{zhang2025safevla} and test-time filters: control barrier functions~\cite{hu2025vlsa}, collision risk gating~\cite{cofreeevla2026}, inverse kinematics QP~\cite{spalter2024ikinqp}, and diffusion-process collision costs~\cite{li2024langguided,yang2025cape}. A recent survey~\cite{li2026vlasafety} categorizes these into runtime shields, constrained optimization, and representation-based methods. Common to all is that safety is imposed externally rather than learned within the policy's representations. This work tests whether auxiliary SDF supervision can produce intrinsic collision awareness.
8	
9	\paragraph{Signed Distance Functions in Robotics.}
10	SDFs encode minimum surface distance with sign indicating free space or penetration. Neural SDFs~\cite{park2019deepsdf} enable continuous shape representation; in robotics, SDFs serve perception~\cite{ortiz2022isdf}, planning~\cite{huang2023voxposer,vasilopoulos2023ramp}, and differentiable collision detection~\cite{zhi2021diffco}. Recent extensions to configuration space~\cite{chen2026cssdfnet} and model predictive control~\cite{jacquet2025neuralnmpc} demonstrate effectiveness in classical pipelines, but integration into end-to-end learned policies remains unexplored. Critically, most methods query the SDF at the end-effector only, leaving full-body collision awareness as an open problem.
11	
12	\paragraph{Evaluation Metrics for Safe Manipulation.}
13	SafeLIBERO~\cite{hu2025vlsa} extends LIBERO~\cite{liu2023libero} with obstacle-augmented environments and a collision avoidance rate (CAR) metric. However, CAR treats all collision-free episodes identically: a frozen robot scores near-perfect CAR without any avoidance behavior. This failure mode echoes concerns about metric reliability~\cite{karwowski2024goodhart,gururangan2018annotation} and binary success metrics under limited samples~\cite{snyder2026beyond}. Action collapse in diffusion policies~\cite{dong2025conditioning} systematically inflates CAR but has not been previously examined as a safety evaluation confounder. We propose dfCAR (\S\ref{sec:dfcar}) to detect this contamination.
14	
```

18	
19	# 论文写作风格规范
20	
21	> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。
22	
23	---
24	
25	## 一、总体写作哲学
26	
27	### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌
28	
29	- 论文必须以"故事线"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证
30	- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进
31	- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌
32	- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing
33	
34	### 1.2 主线统一性
35	
36	- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**
37	- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C
38	- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机
39	- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线
40	
41	### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义
42	
43	- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释
44	- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）
45	- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用
46	- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug
47	
48	### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文
49	
50	- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可
51	- 重点学：
52	  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事
53	  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献
54	  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）
55	  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度
56	- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大
57	- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文
58	
59	---
60	
61	## 二、Abstract 写作规范
62	
63	### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）
64	
65	Abstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：
66	1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点
67	2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）
68	3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）
69	4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献
70	
71	### 2.2 禁忌
72	
73	- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim
74	- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 "revolutionary"、"groundbreaking"
75	- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元
76	- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了
77	- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开
78	
79	### 2.3 审稿人视角检验
80	
81	写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——
82	- 问题是否重要？
83	- 方法是否清楚？
84	- 贡献是否可信？
85	- 结果是否有说服力？
86	
87	---
88	
89	## 三、Introduction 写作规范
90	
91	### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）
92	
93	Introduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：
94	
95	1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈
96	2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题
97	3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）
98	4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题
99	5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献
100	
101	### 3.2 整体叙事结构
102	
103	```
104	现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结
105	```
106	
107	### 3.3 禁忌
108	
109	- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 "LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities..."，要从具体问题切入
110	- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接
111	- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 "我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果" 的罗列
112	- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑
113	- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念
114	
115	---
116	
117	## 四、Related Work 写作规范
118	
119	### 4.1 目标
120	
121	让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。
122	
123	### 4.2 结构要求
124	
125	- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）
126	- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系
127	- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口
128	
129	### 4.3 禁忌
130	
131	- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 "A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z." 的列表
132	- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较
133	- Contribution claim（"To our knowledge, no prior work..."）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work
134	
135	### 4.4 引用充足度
136	
137	- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**
138	- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分
139	- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足
140	
141	---
142	
143	## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）
144	
145	以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**
146	
147	### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）
148	
149	**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**
150	
151	- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**
152	- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子
153	- 强制检查命令：
154	  ```bash
155	  grep -n "—" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要
156	  grep -n " – " paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除
157	  ```
158	- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）
159	
160	### 5.2 段落与句式
161	
162	- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的
163	- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句
164	- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 "..., highlighting the applicability of..." / "..., demonstrating the effectiveness of..."。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一
165	- **禁止连续使用 "Furthermore" / "Moreover" / "Additionally"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词
166	- **避免 "It is worth noting that..." / "Notably, ..."**：直接写要说的内容
167	
168	### 5.3 用词黑名单
169	
170	- 避免 "delve into" / "leverage" / "utilize" / "facilitate" — 用 "explore" / "use" / "enable"
171	- 避免 "a myriad of" / "a plethora of" — 用 "many" / "various"
172	- 避免 "in the realm of" / "in the context of" — 直接说
173	- 避免 "paradigm shift" / "novel" 过度使用
174	
175	### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）
176	
177	1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写
178	2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确
179	3. **`grep "—"` + `grep " – "` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）
180	4. `grep "ing,"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写
181	
182	---
183	
184	## 六、图表与视觉规范
185	
186	### 6.1 知识密度原则
187	
188	- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间
189	- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中
190	- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure
191	
192	### 6.2 表格规范
193	
194	- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）
195	- 小数精度全表统一
196	- 最优加粗、次优下划线
197	- Caption 必须自包含
198	
199	### 6.3 图片规范
200	
201	- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签
202	- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）
203	- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求
204	- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页
205	
206	### 6.4 空间效率
207	
208	- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源
209	- 优先使用 `\subfigure` 合并相关图
210	- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动
211	- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比
212	
213	### 6.5 页面节奏
214	
215	好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：
216	
217	- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉"三步展开
218	- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏
219	- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float
220	- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）
221	- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）
222	
223	### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范
224	
225	**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：
226	- 用 `\toprule` / `\midrule` / `\bottomrule`，禁止 `\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`
227	- 用 `\centering` 不用 `\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）
228	- 最优结果加粗（`\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向
229	- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）
230	- Caption 在表格上方（`\caption` 在 `\begin{tabular}` 之前）
231	
232	**宽度决策树**：
233	- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\columnwidth`）
234	- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）
235	- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix
236	
237	---
238	
239	## 七、投稿前通用准备
240	
241	### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）
242	
243	- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）
244	- **不要用**：
245	  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）
246	  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）
247	  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）
248	- 风险：
249	  - 样式不符合 → desk reject
250	  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信
251	  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范
252	- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch "{venue} {year} latex template official"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）
253	- `\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致
254	
255	### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对
256	
257	- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A
258	- 页数限制：查目标会议官网
259	- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同
260	- 行号开启（review 版常需）
261	- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件
262	
263	### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程
264	
265	提交前必须逐一检查：
266	1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入
267	2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态
268	3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事
269	4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）
270	5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）
271	6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）
272	7. 所有专有名词有定义
273	8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）
274	9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡
275	10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础
276	
````

writing\n  - paper\n  - style\n  - norms\nroles:\n  - agent\n  - worker\n  - paper_agent\n---\n\n# 论文写作风格规范\n\n> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。\n\n---\n\n## 一、总体写作哲学\n\n### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌\n\n- 论文必须以\"故事线\"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证\n- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进\n- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌\n- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing\n\n### 1.2 主线统一性\n\n- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**\n- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C\n- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机\n- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线\n\n### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义\n\n- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释\n- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）\n- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用\n- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug\n\n### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文\n\n- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可\n- 重点学：\n  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事\n  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献\n  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）\n  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度\n- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大\n- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文\n\n---\n\n## 二、Abstract 写作规范\n\n### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）\n\nAbstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：\n1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点\n2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）\n3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）\n4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献\n\n### 2.2 禁忌\n\n- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim\n- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 \"revolutionary\"、\"groundbreaking\"\n- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元\n- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了\n- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开\n\n### 2.3 审稿人视角检验\n\n写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——\n- 问题是否重要？\n- 方法是否清楚？\n- 贡献是否可信？\n- 结果是否有说服力？\n\n---\n\n## 三、Introduction 写作规范\n\n### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）\n\nIntroduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：\n\n1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈\n2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题\n3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）\n4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题\n5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献\n\n### 3.2 整体叙事结构\n\n```\n现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结\n```\n\n### 3.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 \"LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities...\"，要从具体问题切入\n- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接\n- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 \"我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果\" 的罗列\n- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑\n- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念\n\n---\n\n## 四、Related Work 写作规范\n\n### 4.1 目标\n\n让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。\n\n### 4.2 结构要求\n\n- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）\n- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系\n- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口\n\n### 4.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 \"A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z.\" 的列表\n- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较\n- Contribution claim（\"To our knowledge, no prior work...\"）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work\n\n### 4.4 引用充足度\n\n- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**\n- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分\n- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足\n\n---\n\n## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）\n\n以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**\n\n### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n\n**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**\n\n- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**\n- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子\n- 强制检查命令：\n  ```bash\n  grep -n \"—\" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要\n  grep -n \" – \" paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除\n  ```\n- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）\n\n### 5.2 段落与句式\n\n- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的\n- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句\n- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 \"..., highlighting the applicability of...\" / \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一\n- **禁止连续使用 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词\n- **避免 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"**：直接写要说的内容\n\n### 5.3 用词黑名单\n\n- 避免 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"facilitate\" — 用 \"explore\" / \"use\" / \"enable\"\n- 避免 \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\" — 用 \"many\" / \"various\"\n- 避免 \"in the realm of\" / \"in the context of\" — 直接说\n- 避免 \"paradigm shift\" / \"novel\" 过度使用\n\n### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）\n\n1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写\n2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确\n3. **`grep \"—\"` + `grep \" – \"` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）\n4. `grep \"ing,\"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写\n\n---\n\n## 六、图表与视觉规范\n\n### 6.1 知识密度原则\n\n- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间\n- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中\n- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure\n\n### 6.2 表格规范\n\n- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）\n- 小数精度全表统一\n- 最优加粗、次优下划线\n- Caption 必须自包含\n\n### 6.3 图片规范\n\n- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签\n- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）\n- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求\n- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页\n\n### 6.4 空间效率\n\n- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源\n- 优先使用 `\\subfigure` 合并相关图\n- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动\n- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比\n\n### 6.5 页面节奏\n\n好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：\n\n- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用\"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉\"三步展开\n- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏\n- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float\n- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）\n- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）\n\n### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范\n\n**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：\n- 用 `\\toprule` / `\\midrule` / `\\bottomrule`，禁止 `\\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`\n- 用 `\\centering` 不用 `\\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）\n- 最优结果加粗（`\\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向\n- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）\n- Caption 在表格上方（`\\caption` 在 `\\begin{tabular}` 之前）\n\n**宽度决策树**：\n- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\\columnwidth`）\n- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）\n- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix\n\n---\n\n## 七、投稿前通用准备\n\n### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）\n\n- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）\n- **不要用**：\n  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）\n  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）\n  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）\n- 风险：\n  - 样式不符合 → desk reject\n  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信\n  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范\n- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch \"{venue} {year} latex template official\"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）\n- `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致\n\n### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对\n\n- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A\n- 页数限制：查目标会议官网\n- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同\n- 行号开启（review 版常需）\n- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件\n\n### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程\n\n提交前必须逐一检查：\n1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入\n2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态\n3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事\n4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）\n5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）\n6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）\n7. 所有专有名词有定义\n8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）\n9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡\n10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础\n",
      "numLines": 276,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 276
    }
  }
}

I'm working through the phrasing of this result about per-task avoidance patterns. The key insight is that patterns remain consistent across conditions, which suggests collision avoidance comes from the demonstration data itself rather than from end-effector-only supervision. Now I'm looking for common AI-writing patterns like "It is worth noting," "Interestingly," and "-ing," constructions that might need revision. The SDF supervision actually hurts performance at inference time—all oracle variants drop below the unrefined baseline because the gradients push the end-effector away from obstacles but drive the arm links into them instead. During training, the effect is dose-dependent: LoRA fine-tuning alone gets us to 54.83%, matching what we see with light SDF weighting, though higher weights show marginal improvements that still follow the demonstration distribution rather than learning true collision avoidance.

The key insight is that per-task avoidance patterns stay consistent regardless of whether we use SDF supervision—the model learns to avoid collisions from the demonstration data itself, not from the geometric constraints. This means displacement filtering is essential for any meaningful safety evaluation, since end-effector-only supervision can't actually teach collision avoidance and shouldn't be credited with it.

The metric name itself isn't a number, so correcting it feels like legitimate text polishing rather than data manipulation. But I want to be conservative here, so I'll flag this discrepancy without making the change.

Now I'm moving through the introduction section more carefully, checking the citations and claims about VLA safety benchmarks and recent methods in collision avoidance.

The opening sentence about VLA models needing to avoid collisions is clean. The next part lists several recent approaches—SDF-augmented training, constraint-based planning, collision-free demonstrations—but it's a long sentence with a parenthetical that disrupts the flow. The follow-up about our SDF-augmented Pi0 reaching 91.5% CAR is punchy and effective.

Further down, there's a dramatic reveal showing that despite the high CAR metric, the robot is actually frozen with zero task success rate—the collision avoidance is real but the system isn't actually accomplishing anything. This pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations.

The key insight is that we're identifying what we call "fake safety"—where action collapse (near-zero movements that trivially avoid collisions) makes the reported CAR misleading, and standard metrics can't tell the difference between inaction and genuine avoidance. Then we scope out our investigation: we tested this across Pi0 and Pi0.5 models on SafeLIBERO with over 100 experiments and 6000 episodes, and three main findings emerged. The first claim introduces displacement-filtered CAR, which filters out episodes below a certain movement threshold to reveal whether the model is actually doing anything.

When we apply this filter, Pi0's reported safety drops dramatically from 91.5% down to nearly zero—only 3 out of 732 safe episodes survive the filter. The bimodal distribution of displacements makes this threshold robust across a huge range of values. Pi0.5 confirms the pattern: it shows 74.7% task success and 26.8% raw CAR, but every safe episode involves substantial displacement, so the filtered metric matches the raw one. The second claim shows something counterintuitive: even oracle variants that know the true obstacles achieve lower safety scores than the unrefined baselines, because the signed distance field gradient pushes the end effector away from obstacles but rotates the arm itself into collision volumes.

Now I'm looking at the third claim about fine-tuning. LoRA training on Pi0.5 boosts CAR by roughly 28 percentage points, jumping from 26.8% to 54.83%, even without any frozen episodes. The per-task avoidance patterns look identical whether we use geometric supervision or not, which suggests the collision-free demonstrations themselves drive the improvement, not the SDF signal. Looking at the intro section, I should fix the "-ing pattern" issue in the third claim paragraph and maybe tighten that long opening sentence, but overall the narrative flows well so I'll keep structural changes minimal. The opening of claim1.tex is strong—it establishes that standard CAR's core assumption breaks down systematically, with over 99% of collision-free episodes in Pi0 being action-collapse artifacts rather than genuine safety. When dfCAR is applied, these collapse to near zero, which cleanly validates the metric. The Pi0+A1 case is particularly effective because it demonstrates the irony: the highest raw CAR score actually comes from the most severe action collapse, a parameter mismatch that prevents proper LoRA loading. Then Pi0.5 provides the inverse validation—every safe episode shows substantial displacement, confirming the metric works from both directions.

The quadrant analysis is well-structured: Pi0-family models cluster in the upper-left (high CAR but near-zero TSR), while Pi0.5 sits in the lower-right (high TSR, low CAR). The upper-right quadrant represents genuinely safe behavior, whereas the upper-left is accessible purely through action collapse. The displacement distribution itself is sharply bimodal with a five-order-of-magnitude gap, making the threshold robust.

Now I'm noticing two grammatical issues to fix: the "-ing" pattern in "yielding dfCAR" and "revealing that" both need restructuring. Once I correct those in claim1, I should move to claim2 and examine the oracle experiment results—it's showing that even with perfect distance field information, the oracle variants underperform the baselines, which is a surprising finding worth highlighting.

For claim2, the oracle setup is clean, but there's another "-ing" construction in "trading one collision for another" that needs rewriting. The core insight is solid though: EE-only refinement fails because it redistributes collisions to unmonitored arm links rather than solving the problem, regardless of signal quality.

Now looking at claim3, I'm examining how collision-free demonstrations alone drive the avoidance gains. The rhetorical question sets up nicely—does SDF supervision add anything beyond what the demonstrations already provide? The comparison across LoRA-only, LoRA+SDF at two lambda values gives a clean experimental structure, and the table shows demonstrations alone jump CAR by roughly 28 percentage points with no frozen episodes.

The key finding is that adding SDF supervision doesn't help—LoRA-only stays within 0.25 percentage points of the lambda=0.005 variant and is statistically indistinguishable from lambda=0.01. The Phase 2 ablation where SDF is zeroed out after step 5K confirms this: the geometric supervision leaves no lasting behavioral imprint. I'm noticing the writing uses "-ing" constructions ("confirming") a couple times, which is fine stylistically but worth being aware of for consistency.

Looking at the per-task CAR ordering, it's completely stable across all conditions—chocolate pudding stays highest, BBQ sauce stays lowest—and doesn't shift with lambda across a 20x range. This tells me the avoidance patterns are baked into the demonstration distribution itself, not something the geometric supervision is driving. The CAR-TSR trade-off is the real story: fine-tuning tanks TSR by about 21-25 percentage points (from 74.7% down to 49.7-54%), but this is a distributional shift from the pre-trained prior, not learned paralysis—all models still move more than 0.37 meters and have zero frozen episodes. The SSR/TSR ratio climbs from 0.33 in the baseline up to 0.51-0.57 with fine-tuning.

When I look at the higher lambda value of 0.1, CAR jumps to 58.25% at the 30K checkpoint (though only two of three seeds hit this), which edges above the null distribution mean of 54.67%. The z-score of 1.76 gives a p-value around 0.039, so there's a signal there, but with just n=2 it's suggestive rather than conclusive. I'm starting to examine the stepwise ablation results to see what's actually driving these changes. I'm reviewing the discussion section now. The opening effectively frames the core finding—that current VLA safety evaluation has structural reliability issues—supported by three converging results: action collapse undermines reported CAR metrics, EE-only oracle refinement backfires, and collision-free demonstrations rather than geometric supervision drive the avoidance improvements. The explanation for why EE-only SDF fails identifies two independent mechanisms, with the first being that demonstrations themselves encode spatial avoidance implicitly, accounting for the full ~28 percentage point CAR gain. I need to clarify what "these levels" refers to—it seems like it should be "these two causes" or "these factors" to make the independence claim clearer. The rest of the implications section flows well: dfCAR addresses the action-collapse issue using readily available EE position data, the bimodal distribution keeps the metric stable across a wide range, and reporting both metrics reveals the contamination magnitude, which spans thousands of orders of magnitude in parameter space.

For limitations, I'm noting that the evaluation is appropriately scoped to one benchmark and two model families, the tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulators may not transfer to mobile platforms or complex scenes, and the oracle experiments used a largely frozen model which means testing on an actively learning policy would better isolate whether this is a fundamental structural issue.

The lambda condition with only two seeds shows marginal statistical significance that's inconclusive, full-body SDF hasn't been tested yet, and while fine-tuning drops task success rate by about 21-25 percentage points, the fact that success-and-safety rate improves from 0.33 to 0.51-0.57 rules out reduced exposure as the explanation.

For future work, the three main directions are implementing full-body SDF supervision along the kinematic chain, evaluating across other vision-language action models like OpenVLA and RT-2, and moving safety constraints from end-effector space to the planning layer in joint space.

On broader impact, the key insight is that contaminated CAR scores let unsafe models pass safety benchmarks, and the proposed dfCAR metric exposes this vulnerability with minimal protocol changes.

Now I'm reviewing the related work section—the opening establishes that while recent work adds 3D spatial reasoning through auxiliary supervision, safety considerations are largely missing since most VLAs train on collision-free data and only measure task success without distinguishing genuine avoidance from incidental safety. The safety-aware approaches span both training-time constrained reinforcement learning and test-time filtering methods like control barrier functions and collision risk gating.

The key distinction here is that existing safety mechanisms are externally imposed rather than learned within the policy itself, so this work investigates whether auxiliary SDF supervision can develop intrinsic collision awareness. SDFs represent minimum surface distance with sign information to distinguish free space from penetration.

Neural SDFs enable continuous shape representation and have proven useful for perception, planning, and differentiable collision detection in robotics. Recent work extends them to configuration space and model predictive control, showing promise in classical pipelines, though their integration into end-to-end learned policies hasn't been explored yet. A critical gap is that most approaches only query the SDF at the end-effector, leaving full-body collision awareness unaddressed.

SafeLIBERO builds on LIBERO by adding obstacle-augmented environments and a collision avoidance rate metric, but this metric has a fundamental flaw: it treats all collision-free episodes equally, so a frozen robot can achieve near-perfect scores without actually avoiding anything. This echoes broader concerns about metric reliability and the limitations of binary success measures with small sample sizes. Action collapse in diffusion policies systematically inflates the metric but hasn't been studied as a confounding factor in safety evaluation, which is why I'm proposing dfCAR to identify and correct this contamination.

For the method section, I'm evaluating on SafeLIBERO's object_pudding suite with four tabletop pick-and-place tasks using per-timestep collision detection. I'm testing two VLA families—Pi0 with flow matching and Pi0.5 with broader pre-training—across 50 episodes per task, with Pi0.5 running multiple seeds for robustness.

I'm reporting two key metrics: Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR) as the fraction of collision-free episodes, and Task Success Rate (TSR) as the task-completion fraction. I notice the standard CAR metric can be misleading because it conflates genuine avoidance with action collapse, so I'm defining action-collapsed episodes as those where maximum displacement falls below 0.1 mm and excluding them from analysis. The displacement distribution shows a sharp bimodal pattern—collapsed episodes cluster below 1 micrometer while active episodes sit above 200 micrometers.

I'm augmenting LoRA-adapted VLAs with an SDF decoder and end-effector position extractor. In the Pi0+A1 configuration, a parameter-name mismatch during LoRA loading triggers action collapse entirely. To establish an upper bound on end-effector-only refinement, I'm also testing a variant that replaces the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry.

Now I've identified the key edits needed across the paper—roughly 24 total—spanning the abstract, introduction, three main claims, discussion, related work, and method sections. I'll start systematically working through the abstract edits first, then proceed chapter by chapter.

For the abstract specifically, I'm preparing the Edit calls to make sure each old_string is unique in the file. The changes include replacing the em dash with a semicolon for better contrast, adding the (EE) acronym definition, simplifying "Our analysis further reveals that" to "We show that," and fixing the grammar around how SDF gradients interact with the end-effector and arm links.

I also need to remove some unnecessary -ing constructions and fix the dfCAR notation to just CAR to be consistent with the rest of the paper. But I'm second-guessing myself on the oracle experiments context—I need to clarify whether the metric discussion in the abstract is really about inference-time behavior or something else.

Looking at the actual data from the oracle table, the CAR values are what's being reported directly, not dfCAR. Since the Pi0 models have such high fake safety rates, dfCAR would essentially be zero across the board, which wouldn't make sense for the comparison being made. So changing dfCAR to CAR is the right call here.

Now I'm ready to make the edits. I've identified two separate changes in the same sentence that I can handle independently since each has a unique string to target: the dfCAR reference and the grammar fix about SDF gradients. Let me write out all the edits. Looking at the empirical results more closely—SDF supervision actually hurts performance at inference time across all oracle variants, with the end-effector repulsion gradients pushing arm links into obstacles. During training, though, the effect is dose-dependent: LoRA fine-tuning alone gets dfCAR to 54.83%, which matches what SDF augmentation achieves at lower lambda values, and only at higher lambda (0.1) do we see marginal improvements in two of three seeds with specific per-task patterns emerging.

But here's the key finding: those per-task avoidance patterns stay consistent regardless of whether we use SDF supervision or not, which suggests the model is actually learning collision avoidance from the demonstration data itself rather than from the geometric constraints. This means displacement filtering is critical for properly evaluating VLA safety—end-effector-only geometric supervision can't be credited with producing collision avoidance if the behavior emerges from the demonstrations alone.

Now I'm noticing an inconsistency in the abstract around the metric terminology. The abstract uses "dfCAR" when discussing LoRA fine-tuning results, but the claim section and Table 3 refer to "CAR" instead. For Pi0.5, these are equivalent since there's no action collapse, so dfCAR equals CAR. However, for the oracle experiments on Pi0 where frozen episodes exist, using dfCAR is misleading because that metric would be near zero for both conditions. I should standardize the terminology—keeping dfCAR in the LoRA sentence since that's where the metric was introduced, but switching to CAR for the oracle comparison to accurately reflect what's being measured.

I'm also going through the abstract systematically to tighten the language and fix some structural issues. The edits include replacing the em-dash with a semicolon, adding the "(EE)" abbreviation after the first mention of end-effector, rephrasing "Our analysis further reveals that" to the more direct "We show that," and adjusting the phrasing around how SDF gradients interact with the arm to be more precise. I'm also removing some filler language and fixing the grammar around the collision avoidance mechanism. Before finalizing, I want to verify that all the scope qualifiers are properly established—checking that SafeLIBERO is introduced, the Pi0 family is specified, and the end-effector-only constraint is clear.

Now I'm moving through the introduction to check whether the scope is adequately set there as well. The text mentions the Pi0 and Pi0.5 model families on SafeLIBERO, which is good. There's a bold claim about "Fake safety is pervasive," and while it might seem like overclaiming at first, the paragraph that follows properly scopes it to the specific benchmark and configurations tested, so I'll leave it as is.

I've verified the scope throughout and now I'm ready to start making edits, beginning with the abstract.

    "originalFile": "\\begin{abstract}\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models report high Collision Avoidance Rates (CAR) on safety benchmarks, yet we show these metrics are contaminated by action collapse.\nOn SafeLIBERO, an SDF-augmented Pi0 achieves 91.5\\% CAR while 99.6\\% of its safe episodes exhibit end-effector (EE) displacement below 0.1\\,mm; the robot is frozen, not safe.\nWe present displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes sub-threshold episodes, as a corrective metric; the bimodal displacement distribution makes it robust across a $[2, 200]$\\,mm threshold range.\nWe show that end-effector-only Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision is neither necessary nor structurally sufficient for collision avoidance.\nIt is counterproductive at inference time: three ground-truth oracle variants all reduce dfCAR below unrefined baselines, as SDF gradients repelling the end-effector drive arm links into obstacles.\nIts contribution during training is dose-dependent: LoRA-only fine-tuning raises dfCAR from 26.8\\% to 54.83\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$2.25, matching SDF-augmented training at $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (54.5\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$2.29) and $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (56.33\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$1.61); at $\\lambda{=}0.1$, two of three seeds show a marginal signal (58.25\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$0.35, $p{\\approx}0.039$) whose per-task avoidance pattern still mirrors the demonstration distribution, requiring further investigation.\nPer-task avoidance patterns remain identical across all conditions, indicating that collision avoidance arises from collision-free demonstration data rather than EE-only geometric supervision.\nDisplacement filtering is necessary for meaningful VLA safety evaluation; EE-only geometric supervision can be neither credited with nor expected to deliver collision avoidance.\n\\end{abstract}",

_source line 65 · 2026-05-27T20:18:29.467Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012QD6NEtM6vZRUehH13oKx7`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models report high Collision Avoidance Rates (CAR) on safety benchmarks, yet we show these metrics are contaminated by action collapse.
3	On SafeLIBERO, an SDF-augmented Pi0 achieves 91.5\% CAR while 99.6\% of its safe episodes exhibit end-effector (EE) displacement below 0.1\,mm; the robot is frozen, not safe.
4	We present displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes sub-threshold episodes, as a corrective metric; the bimodal displacement distribution makes it robust across a $[2, 200]$\,mm threshold range.
5	We show that end-effector-only Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision is neither necessary nor structurally sufficient for collision avoidance.
6	It is counterproductive at inference time: three ground-truth oracle variants all reduce CAR below unrefined baselines, as the SDF gradient repels the end-effector but drives arm links into obstacles.
7	Its contribution during training is dose-dependent: LoRA-only fine-tuning raises dfCAR from 26.8\% to 54.83\%$\,\pm\,$2.25, matching SDF-augmented training at $\lambda{=}0.005$ (54.5\%$\,\pm\,$2.29) and $\lambda{=}0.01$ (56.33\%$\,\pm\,$1.61); at $\lambda{=}0.1$, two of three seeds show a marginal signal (58.25\%$\,\pm\,$0.35, $p{\approx}0.039$) whose per-task avoidance pattern still mirrors the demonstration distribution.
8	Per-task avoidance patterns remain identical across all conditions: collision avoidance arises from collision-free demonstration data rather than EE-only geometric supervision.
9	Displacement filtering is necessary for meaningful VLA safety evaluation; EE-only geometric supervision can be neither credited with nor expected to deliver collision avoidance.
10	\end{abstract}
```

</details>

    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:intro}\n\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models must avoid collisions to operate safely in physical environments~\\cite{li2026vlasafety}. The SafeLIBERO benchmark~\\cite{hu2025vlsa} provides the first systematic protocol for measuring collision avoidance in VLA manipulation, and recent methods, including SDF-augmented training~\\cite{zhang2025safevla}, constraint-based planning~\\cite{yang2025cape}, and collision-free demonstrations~\\cite{cofreeevla2026}, report high Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). Our SDF-augmented Pi0~\\cite{black2024pi0} reaches 91.5\\% CAR across 800 episodes. The problem appears close to solved.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig1_teaser}\n\\caption{The fake safety problem in VLA collision avoidance evaluation. Standard CAR classifies a frozen robot as safe because it produces zero collisions (left). dfCAR filters out action-collapsed episodes where displacement falls below $\\tau_d$, revealing that Pi0+A1's 91.5\\% safety score drops to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (center). Pi0.5 exhibits genuine avoidance with active motion above 288\\,mm in all safe episodes, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (right).}\n\\label{fig:teaser}\n\\end{figure}\n\nIt is not (Figure~\\ref{fig:teaser}). Of the 732 collision-free episodes, 99.6\\% exhibit displacement below 0.1\\,mm; TSR is 0\\% across all 800 episodes. The robot is frozen, not safe. The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \\emph{fake safety}: \\emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.\n\nWe present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.\n\n\\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\\% to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\\,=\\,74.7\\%, CAR\\,=\\,26.8\\%, with every safe episode above 288\\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}).\n\n\\textbf{EE-only SDF refinement is structurally counterproductive.} Three ground-truth oracle variants all achieve \\emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines (73.1--76.9\\% vs.\\ 82--83\\%). The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}).\n\n\\textbf{Collision-free demonstrations are sufficient for collision avoidance.} LoRA fine-tuning on Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\sim}$28 points (26.8\\% $\\to$ 54.83\\%) with zero frozen episodes; adding SDF supervision at low loss weights ($\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01\\}$) produces no measurable benefit and at high $\\lambda = 0.1$ yields only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($p \\approx 0.039$, $n = 2$); the per-task avoidance pattern is identical with and without geometric supervision, indicating that the collision-free demonstration distribution, not EE-only SDF supervision, accounts for all observed avoidance learning on this benchmark (Section~\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{dfCAR metric:} displacement-filtered CAR that separates genuine avoidance from action collapse, robust across $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm.\n    \\item \\textbf{Fake safety diagnosis:} 100+ experiments across Pi0/Pi0.5 showing $>$99\\% of Pi0 safe episodes are action-collapse artifacts.\n    \\item \\textbf{EE-only SDF is counterproductive:} ground-truth oracle refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR below unrefined baselines by redistributing collisions to unmonitored arm links.\n    \\item \\textbf{Demonstration sufficiency:} on SafeLIBERO, collision-free demonstrations account for all measured CAR gains (${\\sim}$28\\,pp); EE-only SDF at low $\\lambda$ ($\\leq 0.01$) adds nothing, and at high $\\lambda = 0.1$ shows only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($n = 2$) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.\n\\end{itemize}",

    "originalFile": "\\section{The Fake Safety Problem}\\label{sec:fake_safety}\n\nStandard CAR assumes that collision-free episodes reflect deliberate avoidance. This assumption is systematically violated: in every Pi0 configuration tested, over 99\\% of collision-free episodes are action-collapse artifacts (Table~\\ref{tab:fake_safety}).\\footnote{Pi0-family values report obstacle displacement from the original protocol; Pi0.5 reports EE displacement from the rotation-corrected protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:dfcar}). Pi0+A1 achieves the \\emph{highest} raw CAR (91.5\\%) because more complete action collapse (from a parameter-name mismatch preventing LoRA loading; Table~\\ref{tab:configurations}) yields higher fake safety. Pi0.5 validates the metric from the opposite direction: every safe episode exceeds 200\\,mm displacement, yielding dfCAR equal to raw CAR (26.8\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,1.6). The displacement distribution is sharply bimodal (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_displacement}), with a five-order-of-magnitude gap that makes $\\tau_d$ robust.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Fake safety decomposition on SafeLIBERO. dfCAR excludes episodes with displacement $< \\tau_d = 0.1$\\,mm.}\n\\label{tab:fake_safety}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Ep. & CAR (\\%) & TSR (\\%) & Fake Safe (\\%) & dfCAR (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0 Full-FT  & 200 & 83.0 & 0.5 & 99.4 & $\\approx$\\,0 \\\\\nPi0 LoRA     & 200 & 82.0 & 0.0 & ${\\sim}$99 & $\\approx$\\,0 \\\\\nPi0+A1 (SDF) & 800 & 91.5 & 0.0 & 99.6 & $\\approx$\\,0$^*$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0.5 (3-seed)  & 600 & 26.8\\,$\\pm$\\,1.6 & 74.7\\,$\\pm$\\,1.5 & 0.0 & 26.8 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize $^*$\\,3/732 safe episodes above threshold; denominator too small for reliable rate.}\n\\end{table}\n\nFigure~\\ref{fig:tsr_car_scatter} reveals spatial structure in the TSR-CAR plane.\\label{sec:anticorrelation} Pi0-family models cluster in the upper-left (high CAR, near-zero TSR), Pi0.5 in the lower-right (high TSR, low CAR). The upper-right quadrant (high TSR \\emph{and} CAR) represents genuinely safe behavior; the upper-left is accessible through action collapse alone. Under dfCAR, Pi0 configurations drop to the origin, revealing that the apparent safety-capability tradeoff was an artifact of action-collapse contamination.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\linewidth]{fig_tsr_car_scatter.pdf}\n\\caption{TSR vs.\\ CAR for all configurations.     "originalFile": "\\section{End-Effector-Only SDF Cannot Ensure Full-Body Safety}\\label{sec:ee_only}\n\nCould a perfect SDF signal enable effective EE-only refinement? Table~\\ref{tab:oracle} and Figure~\\ref{fig:oracle_bar} show that all three oracle variants achieve \\emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines: the best oracle (76.9\\%) falls 5.1 points below LoRA (82.0\\%). Refinement with perfect distance information produces more collisions, not fewer.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Oracle SDF refinement with ground-truth distance fields. All GT oracle variants underperform baselines despite perfect distance information.}\n\\label{fig:oracle_bar}\n\\end{figure}\n\nPer-task analysis reveals the mechanism (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}). On the one task where the EE is the primary collision body, oracle refinement helps (+9 points).     "originalFile": "\\section{Avoidance Source Diagnosis}\\label{sec:avoidance_source}\n\nFine-tuning on collision-free demonstrations produces genuine collision avoidance: LoRA-adapted Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\sim}$28 percentage points with zero frozen episodes. Does SDF geometric supervision contribute additional avoidance beyond what demonstrations provide? We compare LoRA-only, LoRA+SDF ($\\lambda = 0.005$), and LoRA+SDF ($\\lambda = 0.01$) on Pi0.5, all evaluated at the 30K-step checkpoint.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Avoidance source attribution on SafeLIBERO. SDF at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ adds no measurable CAR beyond LoRA-only ($p > 0.1$); $\\lambda = 0.1$ shows a marginal signal ($z = 1.76$, $p \\approx 0.039$, $n{=}2$) that requires additional seeds. All fine-tuned models exhibit 0\\% frozen rate and displacement $>$\\,0.37\\,m.}\n\\label{tab:avoidance_source}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Seeds & TSR (\\%) & CAR (\\%) & EE Disp (m) \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0.5 baseline & 3 & 74.7\\,$\\pm$\\,1.5 & 26.8\\,$\\pm$\\,1.6 & 0.367 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA-only (no SDF) & 3 & 49.7\\,$\\pm$\\,3.2 & 54.83\\,$\\pm$\\,2.25 & 0.391 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ & 3 & 54.0\\,$\\pm$\\,3.0 & 54.5\\,$\\pm$\\,2.29 & 0.387 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ & 3 & 53.8\\,$\\pm$\\,1.5 & 56.33\\,$\\pm$\\,1.61 & 0.389 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & 2 & 48.0\\,$\\pm$\\,1.4 & 58.25\\,$\\pm$\\,0.35 & 0.394 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}30K checkpoint, two of three seeds; third seed in progress.}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:avoidance_source} shows that collision-free demonstrations alone raise CAR by ${\\sim}$28 percentage points (26.8\\% $\\to$ 54.83\\%) with zero frozen episodes and displacement above 0.37\\,m, confirming genuine avoidance. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit: LoRA-only CAR (54.83\\%) is within 0.25\\,pp of $\\lambda = 0.005$ and statistically indistinguishable from $\\lambda = 0.01$ (56.33\\%, $p > 0.1$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_null}). A Phase~2 ablation ($\\lambda = 0$ after step 5K) yields CAR\\,=\\,57.5\\%\\footnote{Single seed (s2); computational constraints precluded additional seeds for this ablation.}, confirming that SDF gradients produce no lasting behavioral effect. The per-task CAR ordering is preserved across all conditions (chocolate\\_pudding highest, bbq\\_sauce lowest) and invariant to $\\lambda$ across a 20$\\times$ range, indicating that avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\paragraph{CAR--TSR trade-off.}\\label{sec:tsr_collapse}\nFine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\sim}$21--25\\,pp (74.7\\% $\\to$ 49.7--54.0\\%), reflecting distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\\,0.37\\,m displacement and 0\\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}), confirming that among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.\n\n\\paragraph{Dose-response at higher loss weights.}\\label{sec:lambda_high}\nIncreasing $\\lambda$ by 20$\\times$ to 0.1 yields CAR\\,=\\,58.25\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,0.35 at the 30K checkpoint (two of three seeds), above the null distribution (mean 54.67\\%, $\\sigma = 2.04$, $z = 1.76$, $p \\approx 0.039$). This departure is suggestive but inconclusive at $n = 2$. A stepwise ablation across six checkpoints (16K--28K) shows no sustained trend (mean 55.3\\%, within 0.3$\\sigma$ of the null; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_stepwise}), and per-task CAR ordering at 30K remains identical to all other conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:lambda_car}). The response is thus dose-dependent: SDF is fully redundant at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$), with a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ whose modest effect size (${\\sim}$3.6\\,pp) and limited sample preclude strong conclusions. Even at elevated $\\lambda$, avoidance patterns remain driven by demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\linewidth]{fig5_lambda_curve}\n\\caption{Effect of SDF loss weight $\\lambda$ on CAR and TSR. $\\lambda = 0$: LoRA-only. Low $\\lambda$ ($\\leq 0.01$) remains within the null band; $\\lambda = 0.1$ (58.25\\%, 2 seeds) shows a marginal departure ($p \\approx 0.039$) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.}\n\\label{fig:lambda_car}\n\\end{figure}",

All fine-tuned models exhibit 0\\% frozen rate and displacement $>$\\,0.37\\,m.}\n\\label{tab:avoidance_source}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Seeds & TSR (\\%) & CAR (\\%) & EE Disp (m) \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0.5 baseline & 3 & 74.7\\,$\\pm$\\,1.5 & 26.8\\,$\\pm$\\,1.6 & 0.367 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA-only (no SDF) & 3 & 49.7\\,$\\pm$\\,3.2 & 54.83\\,$\\pm$\\,2.25 & 0.391 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ & 3 & 54.0\\,$\\pm$\\,3.0 & 54.5\\,$\\pm$\\,2.29 & 0.387 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ & 3 & 53.8\\,$\\pm$\\,1.5 & 56.33\\,$\\pm$\\,1.61 & 0.389 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & 2 & 48.0\\,$\\pm$\\,1.4 & 58.25\\,$\\pm$\\,0.35 & 0.394 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}30K checkpoint, two of three seeds; third seed in progress.}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:avoidance_source} shows that collision-free demonstrations alone raise CAR by ${\\sim}$28 percentage points (26.8\\% $\\to$ 54.83\\%) with zero frozen episodes and displacement above 0.37\\,m, which confirms genuine avoidance. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit: LoRA-only CAR (54.83\\%) is within 0.25\\,pp of $\\lambda = 0.005$ and statistically indistinguishable from $\\lambda = 0.01$ (56.33\\%, $p > 0.1$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_null}). A Phase~2 ablation ($\\lambda = 0$ after step 5K) yields CAR\\,=\\,57.5\\%\\footnote{Single seed (s2); computational constraints precluded additional seeds for this ablation.}; SDF gradients produce no lasting behavioral effect. The per-task CAR ordering is preserved across all conditions (chocolate\\_pudding highest, bbq\\_sauce lowest) and invariant to $\\lambda$ across a 20$\\times$ range, indicating that avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\paragraph{CAR--TSR trade-off.}\\label{sec:tsr_collapse}\nFine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\sim}$21--25\\,pp (74.7\\% $\\to$ 49.7--54.0\\%), reflecting distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\\,0.37\\,m displacement and 0\\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}), confirming that among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.\n\n\\paragraph{Dose-response at higher loss weights.}\\label{sec:lambda_high}\nIncreasing $\\lambda$ by 20$\\times$ to 0.1 yields CAR\\,=\\,58.25\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,0.35 at the 30K checkpoint (two of three seeds), above the null distribution (mean 54.67\\%, $\\sigma = 2.04$, $z = 1.76$, $p \\approx 0.039$). This departure is suggestive but inconclusive at $n = 2$. A stepwise ablation across six checkpoints (16K--28K) shows no sustained trend (mean 55.3\\%, within 0.3$\\sigma$ of the null; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_stepwise}), and per-task CAR ordering at 30K remains identical to all other conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:lambda_car}). The response is thus dose-dependent: SDF is fully redundant at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$), with a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ whose modest effect size (${\\sim}$3.6\\,pp) and limited sample preclude strong conclusions. Even at elevated $\\lambda$, avoidance patterns remain driven by demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\linewidth]{fig5_lambda_curve}\n\\caption{Effect of SDF loss weight $\\lambda$ on CAR and TSR. $\\lambda = 0$: LoRA-only. Low $\\lambda$ ($\\leq 0.01$) remains within the null band; $\\lambda = 0.1$ (58.25\\%, 2 seeds) shows a marginal departure ($p \\approx 0.039$) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.}\n\\label{fig:lambda_car}\n\\end{figure}",
