          " Table~\\ref{tab:avoidance_source} shows that collision-free demonstrations alone raise CAR by ${\\sim}$28 percentage points (26.8\\% $\\to$ 54.83\\%) with zero frozen episodes and displacement above 0.37\\,m, which confirms genuine avoidance. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit: LoRA-only CAR (54.83\\%) is within 0.25\\,pp of $\\lambda = 0.005$ and statistically indistinguishable from $\\lambda = 0.01$ (56.33\\%, $p > 0.1$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_null}). A Phase~2 ablation ($\\lambda = 0$ after step 5K) yields CAR\\,=\\,57.5\\%\\footnote{Single seed (s2); computational constraints precluded additional seeds for this ablation.}; SDF gradients produce no lasting behavioral effect. The per-task CAR ordering is preserved across all conditions (chocolate\\_pudding highest, bbq\\_sauce lowest) and invariant to $\\lambda$ across a 20$\\times$ range; avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{CAR--TSR trade-off.}\\label{sec:tsr_collapse}",
          "-Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\sim}$21--25\\,pp (74.7\\% $\\to$ 49.7--54.0\\%), a consequence of distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\\,0.37\\,m displacement and 0\\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}), confirming that among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.",
          "+Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\sim}$21--25\\,pp (74.7\\% $\\to$ 49.7--54.0\\%), a consequence of distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\\,0.37\\,m displacement and 0\\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}): among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Dose-response at higher loss weights.}\\label{sec:lambda_high}",
          " Increasing $\\lambda$ by 20$\\times$ to 0.1 yields CAR\\,=\\,58.25\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,0.35 at the 30K checkpoint (two of three seeds), above the null distribution (mean 54.67\\%, $\\sigma = 2.04$, $z = 1.76$, $p \\approx 0.039$). This departure is suggestive but inconclusive at $n = 2$. A stepwise ablation across six checkpoints (16K--28K) shows no sustained trend (mean 55.3\\%, within 0.3$\\sigma$ of the null; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_stepwise}), and per-task CAR ordering at 30K remains identical to all other conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:lambda_car}). The response is thus dose-dependent: SDF is fully redundant at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$), with a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ whose modest effect size (${\\sim}$3.6\\,pp) and limited sample preclude strong conclusions. Even at elevated $\\lambda$, avoidance patterns remain driven by demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision."
        ]
      }

    "newString": "These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nAcross 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes on two VLA model families, three findings converge: current VLA safety evaluation is structurally unreliable. Action collapse renders reported CAR illusory (\\S\\ref{sec:dfcar}); EE-only oracle refinement is counterproductive (\\S\\ref{sec:ee_only}); and collision-free demonstrations, not geometric supervision, account for all measured avoidance gains (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n\n\\paragraph{Why EE-only SDF fails.}\nTwo independent causes explain the null result. \\textit{(i)~Demonstration data subsumes the SDF signal.} Collision-free demonstrations implicitly encode spatial avoidance; the ${\\sim}$28\\,pp CAR gain arises entirely from this distributional shift, with per-task ordering invariant across all SDF conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). At low $\\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \\textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\\,pp below unrefined baselines (\\S\\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. These levels are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}\ndfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\\sim}$658K trainable), confirming it is a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot.\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations.}\nOur evaluation covers one benchmark suite (SafeLIBERO, four tasks) and two model families (Pi0, Pi0.5). The tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulator and known obstacles may not generalize to mobile platforms and cluttered scenes. Oracle experiments used a largely frozen model (Pi0+A1, TSR${\\approx}$0\\%); testing on an actively moving policy would better isolate the structural limitation. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ condition has $n = 2$ seeds; the marginal departure ($p \\approx 0.039$) is inconclusive. Full-body SDF remains untested. Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\sim}$21--25\\,pp, yet SSR/TSR increases from 0.33 to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}), ruling out reduced exposure as the sole explanation.\n\n\\paragraph{Future work.}\nThree directions follow: (1) full-body SDF supervision along the kinematic chain; (2) cross-architecture evaluation on OpenVLA, RT-2, and other VLA families; (3) planning-layer safety constraints in joint space rather than EE space.\n\n\\paragraph{Broader impact.}\nContaminated CAR scores allow models with no genuine avoidance to pass safety benchmarks. dfCAR exposes this gap with minimal changes to existing protocols.\n",

At low $\\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \\textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. These levels are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nAcross 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes on two VLA model families, three findings converge: current VLA safety evaluation is structurally unreliable. Action collapse renders reported CAR illusory (\\S\\ref{sec:dfcar}); EE-only oracle refinement is counterproductive (\\S\\ref{sec:ee_only}); and collision-free demonstrations, not geometric supervision, account for all measured avoidance gains (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n\n\\paragraph{Why EE-only SDF fails.}\nTwo independent causes explain the null result. \\textit{(i)~Demonstration data subsumes the SDF signal.} Collision-free demonstrations implicitly encode spatial avoidance; the ${\\sim}$28\\,pp CAR gain arises entirely from this distributional shift, with per-task ordering invariant across all SDF conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). At low $\\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \\textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\\,pp below unrefined baselines (\\S\\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}\ndfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\\sim}$658K trainable), a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot.\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations.}\nOur evaluation covers one benchmark suite (SafeLIBERO, four tasks) and two model families (Pi0, Pi0.5). The tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulator and known obstacles may not generalize to mobile platforms and cluttered scenes. Oracle experiments used a largely frozen model (Pi0+A1, TSR${\\approx}$0\\%); testing on an actively moving policy would better isolate the structural limitation. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ condition has $n = 2$ seeds; the marginal departure ($p \\approx 0.039$) is inconclusive. Full-body SDF remains untested. Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\sim}$21--25\\,pp, yet SSR/TSR increases from 0.33 to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}), ruling out reduced exposure as the sole explanation.\n\n\\paragraph{Future work.}\nThree directions follow: (1) full-body SDF supervision along the kinematic chain; (2) cross-architecture evaluation on OpenVLA, RT-2, and other VLA families; (3) planning-layer safety constraints in joint space rather than EE space.\n\n\\paragraph{Broader impact.}\nContaminated CAR scores allow models with no genuine avoidance to pass safety benchmarks. dfCAR exposes this gap with minimal changes to existing protocols.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\\label{sec:related}\n\n\\paragraph{Vision-Language-Action Models.}\nVLA models combine pretrained vision-language backbones with action prediction for generalist manipulation~\\cite{brohan2023rt2,black2024pi0,chi2023diffusionpolicy,kim2024openvla}. Recent work enhances spatial reasoning through auxiliary 3D supervision~\\cite{li2025spatialforcing,li2025qdepth,depthvla2025}, but safety considerations remain largely absent: most VLAs train on collision-free demonstrations and evaluate task success alone, with no mechanism to distinguish genuine avoidance from incidental collision-free trajectories.\n\n\\paragraph{Safety in Robot Learning.}\nSafety-aware approaches span training-time constrained RL~\\cite{zhang2025safevla} and test-time filters: control barrier functions~\\cite{hu2025vlsa}, collision risk gating~\\cite{cofreeevla2026}, inverse kinematics QP~\\cite{spalter2024ikinqp}, and diffusion-process collision costs~\\cite{li2024langguided,yang2025cape}. A recent survey~\\cite{li2026vlasafety} categorizes these into runtime shields, constrained optimization, and representation-based methods. Common to all is that safety is imposed externally rather than learned within the policy's representations. This work tests whether auxiliary SDF supervision can produce intrinsic collision awareness.\n\n\\paragraph{Signed Distance Functions in Robotics.}\nSDFs encode minimum surface distance with sign indicating free space or penetration. Neural SDFs~\\cite{park2019deepsdf} enable continuous shape representation; in robotics, SDFs serve perception~\\cite{ortiz2022isdf}, planning~\\cite{huang2023voxposer,vasilopoulos2023ramp}, and differentiable collision detection~\\cite{zhi2021diffco}. Recent extensions to configuration space~\\cite{chen2026cssdfnet} and model predictive control~\\cite{jacquet2025neuralnmpc} demonstrate effectiveness in classical pipelines, but integration into end-to-end learned policies remains unexplored. Critically, most methods query the SDF at the end-effector only, leaving full-body collision awareness as an open problem.\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation Metrics for Safe Manipulation.}\nSafeLIBERO~\\cite{hu2025vlsa} extends LIBERO~\\cite{liu2023libero} with obstacle-augmented environments and a collision avoidance rate (CAR) metric. However, CAR treats all collision-free episodes identically: a frozen robot scores near-perfect CAR without any avoidance behavior. This failure mode echoes concerns about metric reliability~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart,gururangan2018annotation} and binary success metrics under limited samples~\\cite{snyder2026beyond}. Action collapse in diffusion policies~\\cite{dong2025conditioning} systematically inflates CAR but has not been previously examined as a safety evaluation confounder. We propose dfCAR (\\S\\ref{sec:dfcar}) to detect this contamination.\n",

A recent survey~\\cite{li2026vlasafety} categorizes these into runtime shields, constrained optimization, and representation-based methods. Recent extensions to configuration space~\\cite{chen2026cssdfnet} and model predictive control~\\cite{jacquet2025neuralnmpc} demonstrate effectiveness in classical pipelines, but integration into end-to-end learned policies remains unexplored. Action collapse in diffusion policies~\\cite{dong2025conditioning} systematically inflates CAR but has not been previously examined as a safety evaluation confounder.     "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\\label{sec:related}\n\n\\paragraph{Vision-Language-Action Models.}\nVLA models combine pretrained vision-language backbones with action prediction for generalist manipulation~\\cite{brohan2023rt2,black2024pi0,chi2023diffusionpolicy,kim2024openvla}. Recent work enhances spatial reasoning through auxiliary 3D supervision~\\cite{li2025spatialforcing,li2025qdepth,depthvla2025}, but safety considerations remain largely absent: most VLAs train on collision-free demonstrations and evaluate task success alone, with no mechanism to distinguish genuine avoidance from incidental collision-free trajectories.\n\n\\paragraph{Safety in Robot Learning.}\nSafety-aware approaches span training-time constrained RL~\\cite{zhang2025safevla} and test-time filters: control barrier functions~\\cite{hu2025vlsa}, collision risk gating~\\cite{cofreeevla2026}, inverse kinematics QP~\\cite{spalter2024ikinqp}, and diffusion-process collision costs~\\cite{li2024langguided,yang2025cape}. A recent survey~\\cite{li2026vlasafety} categorizes these into runtime shields, constrained optimization, and representation-based methods. Common to all is that safety is imposed externally rather than learned within the policy's representations. This work tests whether auxiliary SDF supervision can produce intrinsic collision awareness.\n\n\\paragraph{Signed Distance Functions in Robotics.}\nSDFs encode minimum surface distance, where the sign indicates free space or penetration. Neural SDFs~\\cite{park2019deepsdf} enable continuous shape representation; in robotics, SDFs serve perception~\\cite{ortiz2022isdf}, planning~\\cite{huang2023voxposer,vasilopoulos2023ramp}, and differentiable collision detection~\\cite{zhi2021diffco}. Recent extensions to configuration space~\\cite{chen2026cssdfnet} and model predictive control~\\cite{jacquet2025neuralnmpc} demonstrate effectiveness in classical pipelines, but integration into end-to-end learned policies remains unexplored. Critically, most methods query the SDF at the end-effector only, leaving full-body collision awareness as an open problem.\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation Metrics for Safe Manipulation.}\nSafeLIBERO~\\cite{hu2025vlsa} extends LIBERO~\\cite{liu2023libero} with obstacle-augmented environments and a collision avoidance rate (CAR) metric. However, CAR treats all collision-free episodes identically: a frozen robot scores near-perfect CAR without any avoidance behavior. This failure mode echoes concerns about metric reliability~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart,gururangan2018annotation} and binary success metrics under limited samples~\\cite{snyder2026beyond}. Action collapse in diffusion policies~\\cite{dong2025conditioning} systematically inflates CAR but has not been previously examined as a safety evaluation confounder. We propose dfCAR (\\S\\ref{sec:dfcar}) to detect this contamination.\n",

A recent survey~\\cite{li2026vlasafety} categorizes these into runtime shields, constrained optimization, and representation-based methods. Common to all is that safety is imposed externally rather than learned within the policy's representations. This work tests whether auxiliary SDF supervision can produce intrinsic collision awareness.",

Recent extensions to configuration space~\\cite{chen2026cssdfnet} and model predictive control~\\cite{jacquet2025neuralnmpc} demonstrate effectiveness in classical pipelines, but integration into end-to-end learned policies remains unexplored. Critically, most methods query the SDF at the end-effector only, leaving full-body collision awareness as an open problem.",

This failure mode echoes concerns about metric reliability~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart,gururangan2018annotation} and binary success metrics under limited samples~\\cite{snyder2026beyond}. Action collapse in diffusion policies~\\cite{dong2025conditioning} systematically inflates CAR but has not been previously examined as a safety evaluation confounder. We propose dfCAR (\\S\\ref{sec:dfcar}) to detect this contamination."

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experimental Setup and Metrics}\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Benchmark and Models}\\label{sec:benchmark}\n\nWe evaluate on SafeLIBERO~\\cite{hu2025vlsa}, the \\textit{object\\_pudding} suite (Level~I): four tabletop pick-and-place tasks with collision hazards, using per-timestep collision detection. Two VLA families are tested: \\textbf{Pi0} (${\\sim}$2.5B)~\\cite{black2024pi0} with flow matching~\\cite{lipman2023flow}, and \\textbf{Pi0.5} with broader pre-training. Table~\\ref{tab:configurations} lists all configurations. Each uses 50 episodes per task (200 total); Pi0.5 multi-seed runs use 3 seeds (600 episodes). We report Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR), the fraction of collision-free episodes, and Task Success Rate (TSR), the fraction achieving task completion~\\cite{hu2025vlsa,zhang2025safevla}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training configurations. Trainable parameters exclude the frozen backbone.}\n\\label{tab:configurations}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{llr}\n\\toprule\nConfiguration & Description & Trainable Params \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0 Full-FT & Full fine-tune, flow-matching loss & ${\\sim}$2.5B \\\\\nPi0 LoRA & LoRA adapters only & ${\\sim}$33.5M \\\\\nPi0+A1 (SDF aux)\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & LoRA + SDF decoder + EE extractor & ${\\sim}$658K \\\\\nPi0+A3 (learned SDF) & LoRA + SDF decoder w/ gradient refinement & ${\\sim}$34.2M \\\\\nPi0.5 (pre-trained) & No fine-tuning & 0 \\\\\nPi0.5 LoRA & LoRA fine-tune on Pi0.5 & ${\\sim}$33.5M \\\\\nPi0.5+A1 (SDF aux) & LoRA + SDF aux on Pi0.5, $\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\}$ & ${\\sim}$34.2M \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\raggedright\\footnotesize\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}LoRA weights not loaded due to parameter-name mismatch; only auxiliary heads trained.\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Displacement-Filtered CAR}\\label{sec:dfcar}\n\nStandard CAR conflates genuine avoidance with action collapse. We define an episode as \\emph{action-collapsed} when $d_{\\max}(e) < \\tau_d$ ($\\tau_d = 0.1$\\,mm) and exclude such episodes:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:car_f}\n\\text{dfCAR} = \\frac{|\\{e : \\text{safe}(e) \\;\\land\\; d_{\\max}(e) \\geq \\tau_d\\}|}{|\\{e : d_{\\max}(e) \\geq \\tau_d\\}|}\n\\end{equation}\nThe displacement distribution is sharply bimodal, with collapsed episodes below 1\\,$\\mu$m and active episodes above 200\\,mm; any $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm yields identical results (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_threshold}).\\footnote{See Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety} for details on the displacement measurement protocol.}\n\n\\subsection{SDF Auxiliary Training and Oracle Refinement}\\label{sec:sdf_aux}\n\nWe augment LoRA-adapted~\\cite{hu2022lora} VLAs with an SDF decoder and an EE position extractor (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_training}, Table~\\ref{tab:hyperparams}). The total loss is:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:total_loss}\n\\mathcal{L}_\\text{total} = \\mathcal{L}_\\text{action} + \\lambda \\left( \\mathcal{L}_\\text{SDF} + \\mathcal{L}_\\text{EE} \\right)\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\}$. In Pi0+A1, a parameter-name mismatch prevented LoRA loading, causing action collapse (TSR\\,=\\,0\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:configurations}). To test the upper bound of EE-only refinement (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}), we also replace the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry in three oracle variants (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_oracle}).\\label{sec:oracle_setup}\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experimental Setup and Metrics}\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Benchmark and Models}\\label{sec:benchmark}\n\nWe evaluate on SafeLIBERO~\\cite{hu2025vlsa}, the \\textit{object\\_pudding} suite (Level~I): four tabletop pick-and-place tasks with collision hazards and per-timestep collision detection. Two VLA families are tested: \\textbf{Pi0} (${\\sim}$2.5B)~\\cite{black2024pi0} with flow matching~\\cite{lipman2023flow}, and \\textbf{Pi0.5} with broader pre-training. Table~\\ref{tab:configurations} lists all configurations. Each uses 50 episodes per task (200 total); Pi0.5 multi-seed runs use 3 seeds (600 episodes). We report Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR), the fraction of collision-free episodes, and Task Success Rate (TSR), the fraction of episodes that complete the task~\\cite{hu2025vlsa,zhang2025safevla}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training configurations. Trainable parameters exclude the frozen backbone.}\n\\label{tab:configurations}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{llr}\n\\toprule\nConfiguration & Description & Trainable Params \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0 Full-FT & Full fine-tune, flow-matching loss & ${\\sim}$2.5B \\\\\nPi0 LoRA & LoRA adapters only & ${\\sim}$33.5M \\\\\nPi0+A1 (SDF aux)\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & LoRA + SDF decoder + EE extractor & ${\\sim}$658K \\\\\nPi0+A3 (learned SDF) & LoRA + SDF decoder w/ gradient refinement & ${\\sim}$34.2M \\\\\nPi0.5 (pre-trained) & No fine-tuning & 0 \\\\\nPi0.5 LoRA & LoRA fine-tune on Pi0.5 & ${\\sim}$33.5M \\\\\nPi0.5+A1 (SDF aux) & LoRA + SDF aux on Pi0.5, $\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\}$ & ${\\sim}$34.2M \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\raggedright\\footnotesize\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}LoRA weights not loaded due to parameter-name mismatch; only auxiliary heads trained.\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Displacement-Filtered CAR}\\label{sec:dfcar}\n\nStandard CAR conflates genuine avoidance with action collapse. We define an episode as \\emph{action-collapsed} when $d_{\\max}(e) < \\tau_d$ ($\\tau_d = 0.1$\\,mm) and exclude such episodes:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:car_f}\n\\text{dfCAR} = \\frac{|\\{e : \\text{safe}(e) \\;\\land\\; d_{\\max}(e) \\geq \\tau_d\\}|}{|\\{e : d_{\\max}(e) \\geq \\tau_d\\}|}\n\\end{equation}\nThe displacement distribution is sharply bimodal, with collapsed episodes below 1\\,$\\mu$m and active episodes above 200\\,mm; any $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm yields identical results (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_threshold}).\\footnote{See Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety} for details on the displacement measurement protocol.}\n\n\\subsection{SDF Auxiliary Training and Oracle Refinement}\\label{sec:sdf_aux}\n\nWe augment LoRA-adapted~\\cite{hu2022lora} VLAs with an SDF decoder and an EE position extractor (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_training}, Table~\\ref{tab:hyperparams}). The total loss is:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:total_loss}\n\\mathcal{L}_\\text{total} = \\mathcal{L}_\\text{action} + \\lambda \\left( \\mathcal{L}_\\text{SDF} + \\mathcal{L}_\\text{EE} \\right)\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\}$. In Pi0+A1, a parameter-name mismatch prevented LoRA loading, causing action collapse (TSR\\,=\\,0\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:configurations}). To test the upper bound of EE-only refinement (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}), we also replace the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry in three oracle variants (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_oracle}).\\label{sec:oracle_setup}\n",

To test the upper bound of EE-only refinement (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}), we also replace the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry in three oracle variants (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_oracle}).\\label{sec:oracle_setup}",
          "+where $\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\}$. In Pi0+A1, a parameter-name mismatch prevented LoRA loading and caused action collapse (TSR\\,=\\,0\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:configurations}).     "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:intro}\n\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models must avoid collisions to operate safely in physical environments~\\cite{li2026vlasafety}. The SafeLIBERO benchmark~\\cite{hu2025vlsa} provides the first systematic protocol for measuring collision avoidance in VLA manipulation, and recent methods, including SDF-augmented training~\\cite{zhang2025safevla}, constraint-based planning~\\cite{yang2025cape}, and collision-free demonstrations~\\cite{cofreeevla2026}, report high Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). Our SDF-augmented Pi0~\\cite{black2024pi0} reaches 91.5\\% CAR across 800 episodes. The problem appears close to solved.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig1_teaser}\n\\caption{The fake safety problem in VLA collision avoidance evaluation. Standard CAR classifies a frozen robot as safe because it produces zero collisions (left). dfCAR filters out action-collapsed episodes where displacement falls below $\\tau_d$, revealing that Pi0+A1's 91.5\\% safety score drops to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (center). Pi0.5 exhibits genuine avoidance with active motion above 288\\,mm in all safe episodes, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (right).}\n\\label{fig:teaser}\n\\end{figure}\n\nIt is not (Figure~\\ref{fig:teaser}). Of the 732 collision-free episodes, 99.6\\% exhibit displacement below 0.1\\,mm; TSR is 0\\% across all 800 episodes. The robot is frozen, not safe. The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \\emph{fake safety}: \\emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.\n\nWe present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.\n\n\\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\\% to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\\,=\\,74.7\\%, CAR\\,=\\,26.8\\%, with every safe episode above 288\\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}).\n\n\\textbf{EE-only SDF refinement is structurally counterproductive.} Three ground-truth oracle variants all achieve \\emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines (73.1--76.9\\% vs.\\ 82--83\\%). The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}).\n\n\\textbf{Collision-free demonstrations are sufficient for collision avoidance.} LoRA fine-tuning on Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\sim}$28 points (26.8\\% $\\to$ 54.83\\%) with zero frozen episodes; adding SDF supervision at low loss weights ($\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01\\}$) produces no measurable benefit and at high $\\lambda = 0.1$ yields only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($p \\approx 0.039$, $n = 2$); the per-task avoidance pattern is identical with and without geometric supervision. The collision-free demonstration distribution, not EE-only SDF supervision, accounts for all observed avoidance learning on this benchmark (Section~\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{dfCAR metric:} displacement-filtered CAR that separates genuine avoidance from action collapse, robust across $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm.\n    \\item \\textbf{Fake safety diagnosis:} 100+ experiments across Pi0/Pi0.5 showing $>$99\\% of Pi0 safe episodes are action-collapse artifacts.\n    \\item \\textbf{EE-only SDF is counterproductive:} ground-truth oracle refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR below unrefined baselines by redistributing collisions to unmonitored arm links.\n    \\item \\textbf{Demonstration sufficiency:} on SafeLIBERO, collision-free demonstrations account for all measured CAR gains (${\\sim}$28\\,pp); EE-only SDF at low $\\lambda$ ($\\leq 0.01$) adds nothing, and at high $\\lambda = 0.1$ shows only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($n = 2$) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.\n\\end{itemize}",

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}
2	
3	Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models must avoid collisions to operate safely in physical environments~\cite{li2026vlasafety}. The SafeLIBERO benchmark~\cite{hu2025vlsa} provides the first systematic protocol for measuring collision avoidance in VLA manipulation, and recent methods, including SDF-augmented training~\cite{zhang2025safevla}, constraint-based planning~\cite{yang2025cape}, and collision-free demonstrations~\cite{cofreeevla2026}, report high Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). Our SDF-augmented Pi0~\cite{black2024pi0} reaches 91.5\% CAR across 800 episodes. The problem appears close to solved.
4	
5	\begin{figure}[t]
6	\centering
7	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{fig1_teaser}
8	\caption{The fake safety problem in VLA collision avoidance evaluation. Standard CAR classifies a frozen robot as safe because it produces zero collisions (left). dfCAR filters out action-collapsed episodes where displacement falls below $\tau_d$; Pi0+A1's 91.5\% safety score drops to ${\approx}$\,0\% (center). Pi0.5 exhibits genuine avoidance with active motion above 288\,mm in all safe episodes, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (right).}
9	\label{fig:teaser}
10	\end{figure}
11	
12	It is not (Figure~\ref{fig:teaser}). Of the 732 collision-free episodes, 99.6\% exhibit displacement below 0.1\,mm; TSR is 0\% across all 800 episodes. The robot is frozen, not safe. The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \emph{fake safety}: \emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.
13	
14	We present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.
15	
16	\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\% to ${\approx}$\,0\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\,=\,74.7\%, CAR\,=\,26.8\%, with every safe episode above 288\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\ref{sec:fake_safety}).
17	
18	\textbf{EE-only SDF refinement is structurally counterproductive.} Three ground-truth oracle variants all achieve \emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines (73.1--76.9\% vs.\ 82--83\%). The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\ref{sec:ee_only}).
19	
20	\textbf{Collision-free demonstrations are sufficient for collision avoidance.} LoRA fine-tuning on Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\sim}$28 points (26.8\% $\to$ 54.83\%) with zero frozen episodes; adding SDF supervision at low loss weights ($\lambda \in \{0.005, 0.01\}$) produces no measurable benefit and at high $\lambda = 0.1$ yields only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($p \approx 0.039$, $n = 2$); the per-task avoidance pattern is identical with and without geometric supervision. The collision-free demonstration distribution, not EE-only SDF supervision, accounts for all observed avoidance learning on this benchmark (Section~\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).
21	
22	Our contributions are:
23	\begin{itemize}
24	    \item \textbf{dfCAR metric:} displacement-filtered CAR that separates genuine avoidance from action collapse, robust across $\tau_d \in [2, 200]$\,mm.
25	    \item \textbf{Fake safety diagnosis:} 100+ experiments across Pi0/Pi0.5 showing $>$99\% of Pi0 safe episodes are action-collapse artifacts.
26	    \item \textbf{EE-only SDF is counterproductive:} ground-truth oracle refinement \emph{reduces} CAR below unrefined baselines by redistributing collisions to unmonitored arm links.
27	    \item \textbf{Demonstration sufficiency:} on SafeLIBERO, collision-free demonstrations account for all measured CAR gains (${\sim}$28\,pp); EE-only SDF at low $\lambda$ ($\leq 0.01$) adds nothing, and at high $\lambda = 0.1$ shows only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($n = 2$) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.
28	\end{itemize}
```

Our SDF-augmented Pi0~\\cite{black2024pi0} reaches 91.5\\% CAR across 800 episodes. The problem appears close to solved.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig1_teaser}\n\\caption{The fake safety problem in VLA collision avoidance evaluation. The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \\emph{fake safety}: \\emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.\n\nWe present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. 1	\section{The Fake Safety Problem}\label{sec:fake_safety}
2	
3	Standard CAR assumes that collision-free episodes reflect deliberate avoidance. This assumption is systematically violated: in every Pi0 configuration tested, over 99\% of collision-free episodes are action-collapse artifacts (Table~\ref{tab:fake_safety}).\footnote{Pi0-family values report obstacle displacement from the original protocol; Pi0.5 reports EE displacement from the rotation-corrected protocol (\S\ref{sec:dfcar}). Sub-millimeter displacement indicates action collapse in both cases.} Applying dfCAR collapses all three to ${\approx}$\,0\%. Pi0+A1 achieves the \emph{highest} raw CAR (91.5\%) because more complete action collapse (from a parameter-name mismatch preventing LoRA loading; Table~\ref{tab:configurations}) yields higher fake safety. Pi0.5 validates the metric from the opposite direction: every safe episode exceeds 200\,mm displacement, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (26.8\%\,$\pm$\,1.6). The displacement distribution is sharply bimodal (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_displacement}), with a five-order-of-magnitude gap that makes $\tau_d$ robust.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\caption{Fake safety decomposition on SafeLIBERO. dfCAR excludes episodes with displacement $< \tau_d = 0.1$\,mm.}
8	\label{tab:fake_safety}
9	\vspace{2pt}
10	\small
11	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
12	\toprule
13	Method & Ep. & CAR (\%) & TSR (\%) & Fake Safe (\%) & dfCAR (\%) \\
14	\midrule
15	Pi0 Full-FT  & 200 & 83.0 & 0.5 & 99.4 & $\approx$\,0 \\
16	Pi0 LoRA     & 200 & 82.0 & 0.0 & ${\sim}$99 & $\approx$\,0 \\
17	Pi0+A1 (SDF) & 800 & 91.5 & 0.0 & 99.6 & $\approx$\,0$^*$ \\
18	\midrule
19	Pi0.5 (3-seed)  & 600 & 26.8\,$\pm$\,1.6 & 74.7\,$\pm$\,1.5 & 0.0 & 26.8 \\
20	\bottomrule
21	\end{tabular}
22	\vspace{1pt}
23	{\footnotesize $^*$\,3/732 safe episodes above threshold; denominator too small for reliable rate.}
24	\end{table}
25	
26	Figure~\ref{fig:tsr_car_scatter} reveals spatial structure in the TSR-CAR plane.\label{sec:anticorrelation} Pi0-family models cluster in the upper-left (high CAR, near-zero TSR), Pi0.5 in the lower-right (high TSR, low CAR). The upper-right quadrant (high TSR \emph{and} CAR) represents genuinely safe behavior; the upper-left is accessible through action collapse alone. Under dfCAR, Pi0 configurations drop to the origin: the apparent safety-capability tradeoff was an artifact of action-collapse contamination.
27	
28	\begin{figure}[t]
29	\centering
30	\includegraphics[width=0.85\linewidth]{fig_tsr_car_scatter.pdf}
31	\caption{TSR vs.\ CAR for all configurations. Pi0-family models cluster in the upper-left (high CAR from inaction), Pi0.5 in the lower-right (high TSR with collision exposure), fine-tuned Pi0.5 in the center. The upper-right quadrant (high TSR and CAR) represents genuinely safe behavior. Error bars: $\pm$1 std across seeds.}
32	\label{fig:tsr_car_scatter}
33	\end{figure}
34

```text
1	\section{End-Effector-Only SDF Cannot Ensure Full-Body Safety}\label{sec:ee_only}
2	
3	Could a perfect SDF signal enable effective EE-only refinement? We replace the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry (\S\ref{sec:oracle_setup}). Table~\ref{tab:oracle} and Figure~\ref{fig:oracle_bar} show that all three oracle variants achieve \emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines: the best oracle (76.9\%) falls 5.1 points below LoRA (82.0\%). Refinement with perfect distance information produces more collisions, not fewer.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\caption{Oracle SDF refinement with ground-truth distance fields. All oracle variants achieve lower CAR than unrefined baselines. Per-task breakdown in Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}.}
8	\label{tab:oracle}
9	\vspace{2pt}
10	\small
11	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
12	\toprule
13	Method & CAR (\%) & Fake Safe (\%) & Refines/ep \\
14	\midrule
15	LoRA baseline (no refine) & 82.0 & 99.4 & -- \\
16	Full-FT baseline (no refine) & 83.0 & -- & -- \\
17	\midrule
18	Oracle-adaptive (GT, dyn.\ $\alpha$) & 75.0 & 98.3 & 14.2 \\
19	Oracle-fixed (GT, $\alpha{=}0.005$) & 73.1 & 98.3 & 14.8 \\
20	Oracle-feature (GT, feat.\ space) & 76.9 & 96.7 & 241.7 \\
21	\midrule
22	A3 learned SDF & 77.1 & -- & 0.0 \\
23	\bottomrule
24	\end{tabular}
25	\end{table}
26	
27	\begin{figure}[t]
28	\centering
29	\includegraphics[width=0.85\linewidth]{fig_oracle_bar.pdf}
30	\caption{CAR for oracle SDF refinement versus unrefined baselines. All GT oracle variants underperform baselines despite perfect distance information.}
31	\label{fig:oracle_bar}
32	\end{figure}
33	
34	Per-task analysis reveals the mechanism (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}). On the one task where the EE is the primary collision body, oracle refinement helps (+9 points). On the remaining three, the SDF gradient pushes the EE away from obstacles but rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes; the net effect is one collision traded for another. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps per episode). EE-only refinement is structurally counterproductive: joint-space coupling between EE displacement and arm-link rotation redistributes collisions to unmonitored links regardless of signal quality.
```

1	\section{Avoidance Source Diagnosis}\label{sec:avoidance_source}
2	
3	Fine-tuning on collision-free demonstrations produces genuine collision avoidance: LoRA-adapted Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\sim}$28 percentage points with zero frozen episodes. Does SDF geometric supervision contribute additional avoidance beyond what demonstrations provide? We compare LoRA-only, LoRA+SDF ($\lambda = 0.005$), and LoRA+SDF ($\lambda = 0.01$) on Pi0.5, all evaluated at the 30K-step checkpoint.

16	\midrule
17	LoRA-only (no SDF) & 3 & 49.7\,$\pm$\,3.2 & 54.83\,$\pm$\,2.25 & 0.391 \\
18	LoRA+SDF $\lambda{=}0.005$ & 3 & 54.0\,$\pm$\,3.0 & 54.5\,$\pm$\,2.29 & 0.387 \\
19	LoRA+SDF $\lambda{=}0.01$ & 3 & 53.8\,$\pm$\,1.5 & 56.33\,$\pm$\,1.61 & 0.389 \\
20	LoRA+SDF $\lambda{=}0.1$\textsuperscript{$\dagger$} & 2 & 48.0\,$\pm$\,1.4 & 58.25\,$\pm$\,0.35 & 0.394 \\
21	\bottomrule
22	\end{tabular}
23	\vspace{1pt}
24	{\footnotesize \textsuperscript{$\dagger$}30K checkpoint, two of three seeds; third seed in progress.}
25	\end{table}
26	
27	Table~\ref{tab:avoidance_source} shows that collision-free demonstrations alone raise CAR by ${\sim}$28 percentage points (26.8\% $\to$ 54.83\%) with zero frozen episodes and displacement above 0.37\,m, which confirms genuine avoidance. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit: LoRA-only CAR (54.83\%) is within 0.25\,pp of $\lambda = 0.005$ and statistically indistinguishable from $\lambda = 0.01$ (56.33\%, $p > 0.1$; Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_null}). A Phase~2 ablation ($\lambda = 0$ after step 5K) yields CAR\,=\,57.5\%\footnote{Single seed (s2); computational constraints precluded additional seeds for this ablation.}; SDF gradients produce no lasting behavioral effect. The per-task CAR ordering is preserved across all conditions (chocolate\_pudding highest, bbq\_sauce lowest) and invariant to $\lambda$ across a 20$\times$ range; avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.
28	
29	\paragraph{CAR--TSR trade-off.}\label{sec:tsr_collapse}
30	Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\sim}$21--25\,pp (74.7\% $\to$ 49.7--54.0\%), a consequence of distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\,0.37\,m displacement and 0\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}): among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.
31	
32	\paragraph{Dose-response at higher loss weights.}\label{sec:lambda_high}
33	Increasing $\lambda$ by 20$\times$ to 0.1 yields CAR\,=\,58.25\%\,$\pm$\,0.35 at the 30K checkpoint (two of three seeds), above the null distribution (mean 54.67\%, $\sigma = 2.04$, $z = 1.76$, $p \approx 0.039$). This departure is suggestive but inconclusive at $n = 2$. A stepwise ablation across six checkpoints (16K--28K) shows no sustained trend (mean 55.3\%, within 0.3$\sigma$ of the null; Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_stepwise}), and per-task CAR ordering at 30K remains identical to all other conditions (Figure~\ref{fig:lambda_car}). The response is thus dose-dependent: SDF is fully redundant at $\lambda \leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$), with a marginal signal at $\lambda = 0.1$ whose modest effect size (${\sim}$3.6\,pp) and limited sample preclude strong conclusions. Even at elevated $\lambda$, avoidance patterns remain driven by demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.
34	
35	\begin{figure}[t]
36	\centering
37	\includegraphics[width=0.85\linewidth]{fig5_lambda_curve}
38	\caption{Effect of SDF loss weight $\lambda$ on CAR and TSR. $\lambda = 0$: LoRA-only. Low $\lambda$ ($\leq 0.01$) remains within the null band; $\lambda = 0.1$ (58.25\%, 2 seeds) shows a marginal departure ($p \approx 0.039$) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.}
39	\label{fig:lambda_car}
40	\end{figure}
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Across 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes on two VLA model families, three findings converge: current VLA safety evaluation is structurally unreliable. Action collapse renders reported CAR illusory (\S\ref{sec:dfcar}); EE-only oracle refinement is counterproductive (\S\ref{sec:ee_only}); and collision-free demonstrations, not geometric supervision, account for all measured avoidance gains (\S\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).
4	
5	\paragraph{Why EE-only SDF fails.}
6	Two independent causes explain the null result. \textit{(i)~Demonstration data subsumes the SDF signal.} Collision-free demonstrations implicitly encode spatial avoidance; the ${\sim}$28\,pp CAR gain arises entirely from this distributional shift, with per-task ordering invariant across all SDF conditions (\S\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). At low $\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\,pp below unrefined baselines (\S\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.
7	
8	\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}
9	dfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\sim}$658K trainable), a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot.
10	
11	\paragraph{Limitations.}
12	Our evaluation covers one benchmark suite (SafeLIBERO, four tasks) and two model families (Pi0, Pi0.5). The tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulator and known obstacles may not generalize to mobile platforms and cluttered scenes. Oracle experiments used a largely frozen model (Pi0+A1, TSR${\approx}$0\%); testing on an actively moving policy would better isolate the structural limitation. The $\lambda = 0.1$ condition has $n = 2$ seeds; the marginal departure ($p \approx 0.039$) is inconclusive. Full-body SDF remains untested. Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\sim}$21--25\,pp, yet SSR/TSR increases from 0.33 to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}); reduced exposure alone does not explain the pattern.
13	
14	\paragraph{Future work.}
15	Three directions follow: (1) full-body SDF supervision along the kinematic chain; (2) cross-architecture evaluation on OpenVLA, RT-2, and other VLA families; (3) planning-layer safety constraints in joint space rather than EE space.
16	
17	\paragraph{Broader impact.}
18	Contaminated CAR scores allow models with no genuine avoidance to pass safety benchmarks. dfCAR exposes this gap with minimal changes to existing protocols.
19	
```

```text
1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related}
2	
3	\paragraph{Vision-Language-Action Models.}
4	VLA models combine pretrained vision-language backbones with action prediction for generalist manipulation~\cite{brohan2023rt2,black2024pi0,chi2023diffusionpolicy,kim2024openvla}. Recent work enhances spatial reasoning through auxiliary 3D supervision~\cite{li2025spatialforcing,li2025qdepth,depthvla2025}, but safety considerations remain largely absent: most VLAs train on collision-free demonstrations and evaluate task success alone, with no mechanism to distinguish genuine avoidance from incidental collision-free trajectories.
5	
6	\paragraph{Safety in Robot Learning.}
7	Safety-aware approaches span training-time constrained RL~\cite{zhang2025safevla} and test-time filters: control barrier functions~\cite{hu2025vlsa}, collision risk gating~\cite{cofreeevla2026}, inverse kinematics QP~\cite{spalter2024ikinqp}, and diffusion-process collision costs~\cite{li2024langguided,yang2025cape}. A recent survey~\cite{li2026vlasafety} categorizes these into runtime shields, constrained optimization, and representation-based methods. Common to all is that safety is imposed externally rather than learned within the policy's representations. This work tests whether auxiliary SDF supervision can produce intrinsic collision awareness.
8	
9	\paragraph{Signed Distance Functions in Robotics.}
10	SDFs encode minimum surface distance, where the sign indicates free space or penetration. Neural SDFs~\cite{park2019deepsdf} enable continuous shape representation; in robotics, SDFs serve perception~\cite{ortiz2022isdf}, planning~\cite{huang2023voxposer,vasilopoulos2023ramp}, and differentiable collision detection~\cite{zhi2021diffco}. Recent extensions to configuration space~\cite{chen2026cssdfnet} and model predictive control~\cite{jacquet2025neuralnmpc} demonstrate effectiveness in classical pipelines, but integration into end-to-end learned policies remains unexplored. Most methods query the SDF at the end-effector only; full-body collision awareness remains an open problem.
11	
12	\paragraph{Evaluation Metrics for Safe Manipulation.}
13	SafeLIBERO~\cite{hu2025vlsa} extends LIBERO~\cite{liu2023libero} with obstacle-augmented environments and a collision avoidance rate (CAR) metric. However, CAR treats all collision-free episodes identically: a frozen robot scores near-perfect CAR without any avoidance behavior. This failure mode echoes concerns about metric reliability~\cite{karwowski2024goodhart,gururangan2018annotation} and binary success metrics under limited samples~\cite{snyder2026beyond}. Action collapse in diffusion policies~\cite{dong2025conditioning} systematically inflates CAR but has not been previously examined as a safety evaluation confounder. We propose dfCAR (\S\ref{sec:dfcar}) to detect this contamination.
14	
```

1	\section{Experimental Setup and Metrics}\label{sec:method}
2	
3	\subsection{Benchmark and Models}\label{sec:benchmark}
4	
5	We evaluate on SafeLIBERO~\cite{hu2025vlsa}, the \textit{object\_pudding} suite (Level~I): four tabletop pick-and-place tasks with collision hazards and per-timestep collision detection. Two VLA families are tested: \textbf{Pi0} (${\sim}$2.5B)~\cite{black2024pi0} with flow matching~\cite{lipman2023flow}, and \textbf{Pi0.5} with broader pre-training. Table~\ref{tab:configurations} lists all configurations. Each uses 50 episodes per task (200 total); Pi0.5 multi-seed runs use 3 seeds (600 episodes). We report Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR), the fraction of collision-free episodes, and Task Success Rate (TSR), the fraction of episodes that complete the task~\cite{hu2025vlsa,zhang2025safevla}.
6	
7	\begin{table}[t]
8	\centering
9	\caption{Training configurations. Trainable parameters exclude the frozen backbone.}
10	\label{tab:configurations}
11	\vspace{2pt}
12	\small
13	\begin{tabular}{llr}
14	\toprule
15	Configuration & Description & Trainable Params \\
16	\midrule
17	Pi0 Full-FT & Full fine-tune, flow-matching loss & ${\sim}$2.5B \\
18	Pi0 LoRA & LoRA adapters only & ${\sim}$33.5M \\
19	Pi0+A1 (SDF aux)\textsuperscript{$\dagger$} & LoRA + SDF decoder + EE extractor & ${\sim}$658K \\
20	Pi0+A3 (learned SDF) & LoRA + SDF decoder w/ gradient refinement & ${\sim}$34.2M \\
21	Pi0.5 (pre-trained) & No fine-tuning & 0 \\
22	Pi0.5 LoRA & LoRA fine-tune on Pi0.5 & ${\sim}$33.5M \\
23	Pi0.5+A1 (SDF aux) & LoRA + SDF aux on Pi0.5, $\lambda \in \{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\}$ & ${\sim}$34.2M \\
24	\bottomrule
25	\end{tabular}
26	\vspace{2pt}
27	\raggedright\footnotesize\textsuperscript{$\dagger$}LoRA weights not loaded due to parameter-name mismatch; only auxiliary heads trained.
28	\end{table}
29	
30	\subsection{Displacement-Filtered CAR}\label{sec:dfcar}
31	
32	Standard CAR conflates genuine avoidance with action collapse. We define an episode as \emph{action-collapsed} when $d_{\max}(e) < \tau_d$ ($\tau_d = 0.1$\,mm) and exclude such episodes:
33	\begin{equation}\label{eq:car_f}
34	\text{dfCAR} = \frac{|\{e : \text{safe}(e) \;\land\; d_{\max}(e) \geq \tau_d\}|}{|\{e : d_{\max}(e) \geq \tau_d\}|}
35	\end{equation}
36	The displacement distribution is sharply bimodal, with collapsed episodes below 1\,$\mu$m and active episodes above 200\,mm; any $\tau_d \in [2, 200]$\,mm yields identical results (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_threshold}).\footnote{See Section~\ref{sec:fake_safety} for details on the displacement measurement protocol.}
37	
38	\subsection{SDF Auxiliary Training and Oracle Refinement}\label{sec:sdf_aux}
39	
40	We augment LoRA-adapted~\cite{hu2022lora} VLAs with an SDF decoder and an EE position extractor (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_training}, Table~\ref{tab:hyperparams}). The total loss is:
41	\begin{equation}\label{eq:total_loss}
42	\mathcal{L}_\text{total} = \mathcal{L}_\text{action} + \lambda \left( \mathcal{L}_\text{SDF} + \mathcal{L}_\text{EE} \right)
43	\end{equation}
44	where $\lambda \in \{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\}$. In Pi0+A1, a parameter-name mismatch prevented LoRA loading and caused action collapse (TSR\,=\,0\%; Table~\ref{tab:configurations}). To test the upper bound of EE-only refinement (Section~\ref{sec:ee_only}), we also replace the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry in three oracle variants (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_oracle}).\label{sec:oracle_setup}
45	
```

</details>
